# List 10

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>falsehood</b></td><td>/ˈfɔːlshʊd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谎言</td><td>spread a deliberate falsehood<br>散布故意的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He repeated the falsehood so often that people believed it.  他把这个谎言说了太多次，人们竟然信了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deception</td><td>/dɪˈsepʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>欺骗，骗术</td><td>practice deception<br>行骗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He obtained the money by deception and was later arrested.  他骗取了这笔钱，后来被捕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fable</td><td>/ˈfeɪbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>寓言；虚构的故事，谎言</td><td>an ancient fable<br>一则古老的寓言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An ancient fable says the mountain spring is a mother's tears.  一个古老的寓言说，这眼山泉是一位母亲的眼泪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lie</td><td>/ˈlaɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>谎言；说谎</td><td>tell a lie<br>说谎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He told an obvious lie about where he had been.  他就自己去过哪里说了个明显的谎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mendacity</td><td>/menˈdæsəti/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谎言，虚假，不诚实</td><td>shameless mendacity<br>无耻的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters grew tired of the candidate's constant mendacity.  选民厌倦了这位候选人不断的谎言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prevarication</td><td>/prɪˌverɪˈkeɪʃən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>支吾其词，撒谎</td><td>endless prevarication<br>无休止的支吾其词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her answer was pure prevarication rather than an honest denial.  她的回答纯属支吾，而非诚实的否认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 untruth</td><td>/ʌnˈtruːθ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>谎言，虚假</td><td>tell an untruth<br>说谎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was punished less for the untruth than for the cowardice.  他受罚更多是因为怯懦，而非因为谎言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 truth</td><td>/ˈtruːθ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>真相，真实</td><td>tell the truth<br>说出真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took ten years for the truth about the accident to emerge.  这场事故的真相花了十年才浮出水面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 verity</td><td>/ˈverəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>真理，真实</td><td>an eternal verity<br>永恒的真理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play explores eternal verities about love and human loss.  这部戏探讨关于爱与失去的永恒真理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>falter</b></td><td>/ˈfɔːltər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚；犹豫，踌躇</td><td>falter in his resolve<br>决心动摇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mr. Garrison never faltered in his resolve to finish the project.  加里森先生完成这个项目的决心从未动摇。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lurch</td><td>/lɜːrtʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>蹒跚</td><td>lurch to one side<br>向一侧猛地歪倒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bus lurched to one side and threw passengers against the windows.  公交车猛地向一侧歪倒，把乘客甩到窗户上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stagger</td><td>/ˈstæɡər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>摇晃地走，蹒跚</td><td>stagger to his feet<br>摇摇晃晃站起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exhausted runner staggered across the finish line.  疲惫的跑者摇摇晃晃冲过终点线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stumble</td><td>/ˈstʌmbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>绊倒，跌跌撞撞</td><td>stumble over a root<br>被树根绊倒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stumbled on the dark staircase but did not fall.  她在漆黑的楼梯上绊了一下，但没有摔倒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 teeter</td><td>/ˈtiːtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃，摇摇欲坠</td><td>teeter on the edge<br>在边缘摇摇欲坠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company teetered on the edge of bankruptcy for several months.  公司数月来一直在破产边缘摇摇欲坠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 totter</td><td>/ˈtɑːtər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇摇晃晃地走，蹒跚</td><td>totter across the room<br>摇晃着走过房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The toddler tottered a few steps and then sat down.  幼儿摇摇晃晃走了几步就坐下了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wobble</td><td>/ˈwɑːbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>摇晃，颤抖；犹豫不决</td><td>wobble on the ice<br>在冰上摇晃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old table wobbles whenever someone leans on its left side.  只要有人靠在左边，这张旧桌子就会摇晃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 balance</td><td>/ˈbæləns/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>平衡；使平衡</td><td>keep his balance<br>保持平衡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer kept her balance on a single toe.  舞者用一只脚尖保持平衡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 halt</td><td>/ˈhɔːlt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>停止，暂停</td><td>come to a halt<br>停下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow brought the whole city to a halt.  大雪使整座城市陷入停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hesitate</td><td>/ˈhezəˌteɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>犹豫，迟疑</td><td>hesitate before answering<br>回答前犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not hesitate to call me if the plan changes.  如果计划有变，别犹豫，给我打电话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vacillate</td><td>/ˈvæsəleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>犹豫不决；摇动，摇摆</td><td>vacillate between two options<br>在两个选择间犹豫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Parents who vacillate between approval and refusal confuse their children.  在同意与反对之间摇摆不定的父母会让孩子困惑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 waver</td><td>/ˈweɪvər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>动摇，犹豫不决</td><td>waver between two options<br>在两个选项间摇摆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wavered for weeks before accepting the job offer.  他犹豫了好几周才接受这份工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fanatic</b></td><td>/fəˈnætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂热者</td><td>a fitness fanatic<br>健身狂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My roommate is a fitness fanatic who trains twice a day.  我室友是个健身狂，一天训练两次。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bigot</td><td>/ˈbɪɡət/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>偏执者，固执己见者</td><td>a narrow-minded bigot<br>心胸狭隘的偏执者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a narrow-minded bigot would object to the new neighbors.  只有心胸狭隘的偏执者才会反对新邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devotee</td><td>/ˌdevəˈtiː/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>爱好者，热爱者</td><td>a devotee of opera<br>歌剧爱好者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a devotee of jazz, he attends every festival in the city.  作为爵士乐迷，他参加城里的每一个音乐节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enthusiast</td><td>/ɪnˈθuːziæst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>热衷者，爱好者</td><td>a sports enthusiast<br>体育爱好者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The club is full of cycling enthusiasts who ride every weekend.  俱乐部里全是每周末都骑车的自行车爱好者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fiend</td><td>/ˈfiːnd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>恶魔，残忍之人；狂热者</td><td>a cruel fiend<br>残忍的恶魔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The newspapers described the killer as a heartless fiend.  报纸把这个凶手描述成毫无心肠的恶魔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 freak</td><td>/ˈfriːk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂热爱好者；怪人</td><td>a fitness freak<br>健身狂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My younger brother is a complete film freak.  我弟弟是个十足的电影狂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maniac</td><td>/ˈmeɪniæk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂热者；疯子</td><td>drive like a maniac<br>开车像疯子一样</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a football maniac who never misses a single match.  他是个足球狂，一场比赛都不落。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 partisan</td><td>/ˈpɑːrtəzn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>党派偏见的；盲目拥护者</td><td>partisan politics<br>党派偏见的政治</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The debate quickly sank into partisan shouting about very old grievances.  辩论很快沦为带党派偏见的叫嚷，纠缠陈年旧怨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 zealot</td><td>/ˈzelət/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂热者，极端主义者</td><td>a religious zealot<br>宗教狂热者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a zealot would defend that policy so fiercely.  只有狂热者才会如此激烈地为那项政策辩护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extreme</td><td>/ɪkˈstriːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>极端的，极度的；极端</td><td>extreme views<br>极端的观点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His extreme views cost him most of his old friends.  他极端的观点让他失去了大部分老朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rabid</td><td>/ˈræbɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狂热的，不冷静的</td><td>a rabid supporter<br>狂热的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A rabid supporter of the team shouted insults at the referee.  一个狂热的球队支持者对裁判大声辱骂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 radical</td><td>/ˈrædəkəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>激进的；根本的；激进者</td><td>radical reform<br>激进的改革</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council rejected any radical change to the old charter.  议会否决了对旧章程的任何激进改动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 revolutionary</td><td>/ˌrevəˈluːʃəneri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>革命者，激进分子；革命的</td><td>a revolutionary idea<br>革命性的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young revolutionary refused every compromise with the old regime.  这位年轻的革命者拒绝与旧政权做任何妥协。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ultra</td><td>/ˈʌltrə/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>极端主义者；极端的</td><td>a political ultra<br>政治极端分子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few ultras in the party rejected every compromise.  党内少数极端分子拒绝一切妥协。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonfan</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈfæn/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>非爱好者，不感兴趣者</td><td>appeal even to nonfans<br>连非爱好者也吸引</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The movie was so good that even nonfans of the genre enjoyed it.  这部电影好到连不喜欢这类片子的人都看得很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fantasy</b></td><td>/ˈfæntəsi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>幻想</td><td>indulge in fantasy<br>沉溺于幻想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her plan to retire at thirty was pure fantasy.  她三十岁退休的打算纯属幻想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chimera</td><td>/kaɪˈmɪrə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>虚构的事物，幻觉</td><td>a mere chimera<br>纯粹的幻想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lasting peace seemed a mere chimera to the exhausted soldiers.  对疲惫的士兵来说，持久和平仿佛只是幻想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conceit</td><td>/kənˈsiːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>幻想，奇特的想法；自负</td><td>a poetic conceit<br>诗中的奇思妙喻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel rests on the conceit that memories can be traded.  这部小说建立在记忆可以交易的奇想之上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 daydream</td><td>/ˈdeɪˌdriːm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>白日梦，幻想</td><td>lost in a daydream<br>陷入白日梦中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sat by the window lost in a daydream about sailing away.  他坐在窗边，沉浸在扬帆远行的白日梦里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delusion</td><td>/dɪˈluːʒn/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>错觉，妄想</td><td>labor under a delusion<br>抱着错误的幻想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He clung to the delusion that the company would soon rehire him.  他固执地抱着公司很快会重新雇用他的妄想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dream</td><td>/ˈdriːm/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>梦想，幻想；做梦</td><td>dream of fame<br>幻想成名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a child he dreamed of sailing around the world.  他小时候幻想过环球航行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hallucination</td><td>/həˌluːsɪˈneɪʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>幻觉</td><td>suffer hallucinations<br>出现幻觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The high fever gave him vivid hallucinations that lasted through the night.  高烧让他产生了持续整夜的鲜明幻觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 illusion</td><td>/ɪˈluːʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>错觉，幻想</td><td>an optical illusion<br>视觉错觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mirrors create the illusion that the room is twice as large.  镜子造成了房间大了一倍的错觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vision</td><td>/ˈvɪʒən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>幻象，梦幻；视力；远见</td><td>have a vision of<br>看到…的幻象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The saint claimed to have seen a vision of a burning city.  这位圣徒声称看到过一座燃烧城市的幻象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 envisage</td><td>/ɪnˈvɪzɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>设想，想象</td><td>envisage a solution<br>设想一个解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nobody envisaged such a rapid change in the market.  没人设想过市场会有如此迅速的变化。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 envision</td><td>/ɪnˈvɪʒən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>想象，展望</td><td>envision the future<br>展望未来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She envisions a city where nobody needs a car.  她想象着一座无人需要汽车的城市。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fantasize</td><td>/ˈfæntəˌsaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>幻想，想象</td><td>fantasize about winning<br>幻想获胜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He often fantasized about quitting and moving to the coast.  他常幻想辞职搬到海边。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 imagine</td><td>/ˌɪˈmædʒən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>想象；猜想，认为</td><td>hard to imagine<br>难以想象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I imagine the meeting will run past six o'clock.  我猜会议会开到六点以后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 picture</td><td>/ˈpɪktʃər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>想象，设想；图画</td><td>picture himself as a hero<br>想象自己是英雄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Try to picture the room as it looked a century ago.  试着想象这房间一百年前的样子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 actuality</td><td>/ˌæktʃuˈæləti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>现实，实际情况</td><td>in actuality<br>实际上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In actuality the treaty changed almost nothing on the border.  实际上这项条约几乎没改变边境的任何情况。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fact</td><td>/ˈfækt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>事实，实情</td><td>a matter of fact<br>事实问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Whether the letter existed is a question of fact, not opinion.  那封信是否存在是事实问题，不是意见问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reality</td><td>/riˈæləti/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>现实，真实</td><td>face reality<br>面对现实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sooner or later he must face the reality of his debts.  他迟早得面对自己债务的现实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 truth</td><td>/ˈtruːθ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>真相，真实</td><td>tell the truth<br>说出真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took ten years for the truth about the accident to emerge.  这场事故的真相花了十年才浮出水面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>farce</b></td><td>/fɑːrs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>闹剧；滑稽戏；荒唐事</td><td>turn into a farce<br>变成一场闹剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The trial soon turned into a farce that embarrassed the whole court.  这场审判很快变成一场闹剧，让整个法庭都很难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 joke</td><td>/ˈdʒoʊk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>笑话；玩笑</td><td>a bad joke<br>一个糟糕的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole election became a joke to foreign observers.  在外国观察者看来，整场选举变成了笑话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mockery</td><td>/ˈmɑːkəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>笑柄；嘲弄，拙劣的模仿</td><td>make a mockery of justice<br>使正义成为笑柄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rushed trial made a mockery of justice in the eyes of observers.  在观察者看来，这场草率的审判使正义成了笑柄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nonsense</td><td>/ˈnɑːnsens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>胡说，废话</td><td>talk nonsense<br>说废话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of what he wrote in that letter was pure nonsense.  他在那封信里写的大多是纯粹的胡话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fascinate</b></td><td>/ˈfæsɪneɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>强烈吸引，使入迷</td><td>fascinate young readers<br>使小读者着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Stories about strange deep-sea creatures fascinate young readers all over the world.  关于奇异深海生物的故事让全世界的小读者着迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 allure</td><td>/əˈlʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>吸引，诱惑</td><td>the allure of fame<br>名声的诱惑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap rents allure young artists to this run-down district.  低廉的租金吸引年轻艺术家来到这个破旧街区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arrest</td><td>/əˈrest/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>停止；逮捕</td><td>place him under arrest<br>将他逮捕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new drug can arrest the spread of the disease for years.  这种新药能在数年内阻止疾病扩散。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attract</td><td>/əˈtrækt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>吸引，引起</td><td>attract attention<br>吸引注意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exhibition attracted thousands of visitors in its first week.  展览首周吸引了数千名参观者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bewitch</td><td>/bɪˈwɪtʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使着迷；用魔法迷住</td><td>bewitch the audience<br>让观众为之着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her singing bewitched everyone in the crowded little hall.  她的歌声让拥挤小厅里的每个人都着了迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 captivate</td><td>/ˈkæptɪveɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使着迷，深深吸引</td><td>captivate the audience<br>深深吸引观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel captivated readers around the world.  她的第一部小说令世界各地的读者着迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charm</td><td>/ˈtʃɑːrm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>迷住，吸引；魅力</td><td>charm the audience<br>迷住观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist charmed the audience with her first piece.  这位年轻钢琴家用第一首曲子迷住了观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enchant</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃænt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使入迷，使陶醉</td><td>be enchanted by the scenery<br>被风景迷住</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old fishing village enchanted every visitor who stayed there.  那个古老渔村令每位住过的游客陶醉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enthrall</td><td>/ɪnˈθrɔːl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（像用魔咒般）吸引</td><td>enthrall the entire audience<br>吸引住全场观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician enthralled the entire audience with a single deck of cards.  魔术师仅用一副牌就吸引住了全场观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grip</td><td>/ˈɡrɪp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>吸引住；紧握</td><td>grip the reader's attention<br>抓住读者注意力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film gripped us from the very first scene.  这部电影从第一个镜头就抓住了我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disgust</td><td>/dɪsˈɡʌst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使厌恶；厌恶</td><td>disgust even his allies<br>连他的盟友也感到厌恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The state of the kitchen disgusted the health inspector.  厨房的状况让卫生检查员十分厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repel</td><td>/rɪˈpel/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>击退，驱退；使厌恶</td><td>repel the attackers<br>击退进攻者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The greasy smell of the kitchen seemed to repel every customer who entered.  厨房油腻的气味似乎让每个进来的顾客都感到厌恶。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fast</b></td><td>/fæst/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迅速的，快的</td><td>a fast runner<br>跑得快的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a fast worker who finishes every job ahead of schedule.  他做事很快，每项工作都提前完成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hunger strike</td><td>/ˈhʌŋɡər ˌstraɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>绝食抗议</td><td>go on a hunger strike<br>进行绝食抗议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The prisoners began a hunger strike to demand better food.  囚犯们开始绝食抗议，要求改善饮食。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 constant</td><td>/ˈkɑːnstənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>不变的，恒定的；忠诚的</td><td>a constant friend<br>忠诚不变的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her support remained constant through every difficult year.  她的支持在每个艰难年头都不曾改变。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dedicated</td><td>/ˈdedəkeɪtəd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>献身的，尽心的；专用的</td><td>a dedicated teacher<br>尽心尽力的教师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is dedicated to protecting the birds of this marsh.  她致力于保护这片沼泽的鸟类。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devoted</td><td>/dɪˈvoʊtɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>投入的；忠诚的</td><td>a devoted follower<br>忠诚的追随者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is a devoted teacher who remembers every student by name.  她是位投入的教师，能叫出每个学生的名字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devout</td><td>/dɪˈvaʊt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虔诚的，笃信的</td><td>a devout believer<br>虔诚的信徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His devout aunt attended the little chapel every single morning.  他虔诚的姑妈每天早晨都去那间小教堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loyal</td><td>/ˈlɔɪəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忠诚的，忠实的</td><td>a loyal customer<br>忠实的客户</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained loyal to the team even during its worst season.  即使在最糟的赛季，他仍然忠于这支球队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pious</td><td>/ˈpaɪəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（信仰上）虔诚的</td><td>a pious old woman<br>虔诚的老妇人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pious old woman attended the early service every single morning.  那位虔诚的老妇人每天早上都参加早祷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disloyal</td><td>/dɪˈslɔɪəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不忠的，背信的</td><td>disloyal to a friend<br>对朋友不忠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sharing those emails was disloyal to everyone on the team.  分享那些邮件是对团队所有人的背叛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 perfidious</td><td>/pərˈfɪdiəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>背信弃义的，奸诈的</td><td>a perfidious friend<br>背信弃义的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 History remembers him as a perfidious partner in every treaty.  历史记住他是每份条约中背信弃义的伙伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fastidious</b></td><td>/fæˈstɪdiəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>挑剔的，极仔细的，追求完美的</td><td>fastidious about details<br>对细节极挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is fastidious about details and rereads every line twice.  她对细节极为挑剔，每一行都要读两遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demanding</td><td>/dɪˈmændɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难取悦的，难满足的；费时间花心思的</td><td>a demanding new job<br>费心费力的新工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Caring for three small children is demanding work with no holidays.  照顾三个小孩是费时间花心思且没有休假的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exacting</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæktɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严格的，苛求的；费时间花心思的</td><td>exacting quality standards<br>严格的质量标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The airline maintains exacting standards for every part it installs.  这家航空公司对安装的每个部件都保持严格的标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fussy</td><td>/ˈfʌsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挑剔的，过分讲究的</td><td>fussy about small details<br>对小细节很挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old tailor is fussy about the smallest details.  这位老裁缝对最小的细节都很挑剔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hypercritical</td><td>/ˌhaɪpərˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分挑剔的，苛刻的</td><td>a hypercritical reader<br>过分挑剔的读者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her hypercritical remarks discouraged the whole design team.  她过分挑剔的评论让整个设计团队泄气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 squeamish</td><td>/ˈskwiːmɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易恶心的；过分挑剔的</td><td>squeamish about blood<br>见血就恶心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nurses cannot afford to be squeamish about blood or open wounds.  护士不能一见血或伤口就恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undemanding</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈmændɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>要求不高的，轻松的</td><td>an undemanding course<br>要求不高的课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She chose an undemanding role so she could rest more.  她选了个轻松的角色以便多休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fateful</b></td><td>/ˈfeɪtfl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>意义重大的</td><td>a fateful decision<br>意义重大的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That fateful meeting changed the direction of his entire career.  那次意义重大的会面改变了他整个职业生涯的方向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 critical</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪkəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>关键的；批评的</td><td>a critical moment<br>关键时刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water supply is critical to the survival of this town.  供水对这座小镇的存续至关重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crucial</td><td>/ˈkruːʃl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常重要的，决定性的</td><td>a crucial decision<br>关键的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The next two weeks will be crucial to the project's survival.  接下来两周对这个项目的存亡至关重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 important</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpɔːrtənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的</td><td>an important decision<br>一个重要的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sleep is as important as diet for a young athlete.  对年轻运动员来说，睡眠和饮食一样重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 momentous</td><td>/moʊˈmentəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重大的，重要的</td><td>a momentous decision<br>一个重大的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board reached a momentous decision that changed the company's direction entirely.  董事会做出了一个重大决定，彻底改变了公司的方向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 significant</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；显著的</td><td>a significant difference<br>显著的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a significant drop in the pollution levels.  研究者发现污染水平显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inconsequential</td><td>/ˌɪnkɑːnsɪˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，微不足道的</td><td>an inconsequential remark<br>无关紧要的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The difference between the two prices is quite inconsequential.  两个价格之间的差别微不足道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 insignificant</td><td>/ˌɪnsɪɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关紧要的，微不足道的</td><td>a seemingly insignificant change<br>看似微小的改动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An insignificant error early on ruined the entire calculation.  早期一个微不足道的错误毁了整个计算。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 petty</td><td>/ˈpeti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>petty complaints<br>琐碎的抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wasted the meeting on petty complaints about parking spaces.  他把会议浪费在关于停车位的琐碎抱怨上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 paltry</td><td>/ˈpɔːltri/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无价值的；微不足道的</td><td>a paltry sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They offered a paltry sum for a painting worth thousands of dollars.  他们为一幅价值数千美元的画只出了微不足道的一笔钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unimportant</td><td>/ˌʌnɪmˈpɔːrtənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不重要的，无关紧要的</td><td>an unimportant detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the objection as entirely unimportant.  他把这条反对意见视为完全不重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fathom</b></td><td>/ˈfæðəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>彻底理解，弄懂</td><td>fathom his motives<br>弄懂他的动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one could fathom his motives for leaving so suddenly.  没人能弄懂他如此突然离开的动机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apprehend</td><td>/ˌæprɪˈhend/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>逮捕；理解</td><td>apprehend the suspect<br>逮捕嫌疑人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police apprehended the thief two streets from the shop.  警方在离商店两条街的地方逮住了小偷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cognize</del></td><td>/ˈkɑɡnaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>认知，认识到</td><td>cognize the difference<br>认识到差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Infants gradually cognize the difference between faces and objects.  婴儿逐渐认知面孔与物体之间的差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 comprehend</td><td>/ˌkɑːmprɪˈhend/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>理解，了解；包括，包含</td><td>comprehend the full report<br>理解整份报告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few readers can comprehend the full report without expert help.  很少有读者能在没有专家帮助时理解整份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grasp</td><td>/ˈɡræsp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>抓紧，握牢；理解</td><td>grasp the handle<br>抓住把手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grasped the rope tightly and pulled herself up.  她紧抓绳子，把自己拉了上去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 know</td><td>/ˈnoʊ/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>知道，了解</td><td>know the answer<br>知道答案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Few people know how the old machine actually worked.  很少有人知道那台老机器实际是怎么运转的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 understand</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈstænd/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>理解，懂得</td><td>understand the question<br>理解问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I finally understand why the experiment kept failing.  我终于理解实验为何一直失败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 misunderstand</td><td>/ˌmɪsʌndərˈstænd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>误解，误会</td><td>misunderstand the instructions<br>误解说明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Readers often misunderstand the ending of that famous novel.  读者常误解那部著名小说的结尾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fatigue</b></td><td>/fəˈtiːɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>疲惫</td><td>suffer from constant fatigue<br>长期感到疲惫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters battling the blaze all day suffered from severe fatigue.  整天与大火搏斗的消防员疲劳不堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 collapse</td><td>/kəˈlæps/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>失败</td><td>the collapse of talks<br>谈判的破裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The collapse of the talks surprised nobody in the capital.  谈判的破裂在首都没让任何人感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exhaustion</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːstʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>极度疲劳；耗尽</td><td>collapse from exhaustion<br>因极度疲劳而倒下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rescuers worked until exhaustion forced them to rest.  救援人员一直工作到疲劳迫使他们休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lassitude</td><td>/ˈlæsɪtuːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>疲乏，无精打采</td><td>a feeling of lassitude<br>一种倦怠感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A strange lassitude kept him in bed all afternoon.  一种奇怪的倦怠让他整个下午都躺在床上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tiredness</td><td>/ˈtaɪərdnəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>疲劳，疲倦</td><td>a wave of tiredness<br>一阵疲倦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A short nap cleared the tiredness from her eyes.  短暂的小睡消除了她眼中的疲倦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weariness</td><td>/ˈwɪrinəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>疲倦，厌倦</td><td>a growing weariness with politics<br>对政治日增的厌倦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 You could hear the weariness in the old teacher's voice.  你能从老教师的声音里听出疲倦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 refreshment</td><td>/rəˈfreʃmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恢复精力；茶点</td><td>a moment of refreshment<br>片刻的休憩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cold water brought instant refreshment after the long climb.  长途攀登后，冷水立刻让人恢复精力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rejuvenation</td><td>/rɪˌdʒuːvəˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>恢复活力，返老还童</td><td>urban rejuvenation<br>城市复兴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The holiday brought a complete rejuvenation of his spirits.  假期让他的精神完全恢复了活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revitalization</td><td>/ˌriːvaɪtələˈzeɪʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>复兴，重新振兴</td><td>urban revitalization<br>城市复兴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The festival began the revitalization of the sleepy river town.  这个节庆开启了沉寂河镇的复兴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fatuous</b></td><td>/ˈfætʃuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚笨的，昏庸的</td><td>a fatuous remark<br>愚蠢的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fatuous comment showed he had not read the report.  他愚蠢的评论说明他没读过那份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 asinine</td><td>/ˈæsəˌnaɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，荒唐的</td><td>an asinine remark<br>愚蠢的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal was dismissed as an asinine waste of money.  该提案被斥为对金钱的愚蠢浪费。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brainless</td><td>/ˈbreɪnlɪs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，无脑的</td><td>a brainless mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Leaving the keys in the lock was simply brainless.  把钥匙留在锁上真是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 foolish</td><td>/ˈfuːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a foolish mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was foolish of him to ignore the warning signs.  他忽视警示标志实在愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 obtuse</td><td>/əbˈtuːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚钝的</td><td>deliberately obtuse answers<br>故意装糊涂的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave deliberately obtuse answers to every question the inspector asked him.  他对检查员问的每个问题都故意给出装糊涂的回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 silly</td><td>/ˈsɪli/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a silly mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was silly to climb that tall ladder without help.  没人帮忙就爬那么高的梯子太傻了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 simple</td><td>/ˈsɪmpəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简单的，容易的</td><td>a simple task<br>简单的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Follow these simple steps and the machine will restart.  按这些简单步骤操作，机器就会重启。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stupid</td><td>/ˈstuːpəd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，笨的</td><td>a stupid mistake<br>愚蠢的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I felt stupid after mixing up their names again.  又把他们的名字弄混后我觉得自己很笨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unwitty</del></td><td>/ʌnˈwɪti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不机智的，乏味的</td><td>an unwitty joke<br>不好笑的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His unwitty comments fell flat at the dinner party.  他不机智的评论在晚宴上冷场了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 witless</td><td>/ˈwɪtləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，无头脑的</td><td>a witless remark<br>愚蠢的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witless prank got both boys suspended from school.  这个愚蠢的恶作剧让两个男孩都被停学。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sagacious</td><td>/səˈɡeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>睿智的，有远见的</td><td>a sagacious adviser<br>睿智的顾问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sagacious advice saved the family business from certain ruin.  他睿智的建议救家族生意于必败之境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sapient</td><td>/ˈseɪpiənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，有洞察力的</td><td>a sapient observation<br>有洞察力的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few sapient remarks from the editor saved the entire chapter.  编辑几句有洞察力的话救了整整一章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>faultfinder</b></del></td><td>/ˈfɔːltˌfaɪndər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>吹毛求疵的人</td><td>a constant faultfinder<br>总爱挑刺的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team leader was a constant faultfinder who praised nobody.  这位组长总爱挑刺，从不表扬任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carper</td><td>/ˈkɑːrpər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>爱挑剔的人，抱怨者</td><td>an endless carper<br>没完没了的抱怨者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Every committee has one carper who objects to everything.  每个委员会都有一个对一切都反对的挑剔者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 castigator</del></td><td>/ˈkæstəɡeɪtər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>严厉批评者，惩罚者</td><td>a fierce castigator<br>严厉的批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist became the chief castigator of the new law.  这位专栏作家成了新法律的主要严厉批评者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 caviler</del></td><td>/ˈkævələr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无理挑剔的人</td><td>a petty caviler<br>斤斤计较的挑剔者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cavilers attacked the report over a few trivial spelling errors.  挑剔者就几处细微的拼写错误攻击这份报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 censurer</del></td><td>/ˈsɛnʃərər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>谴责者，批评者</td><td>a harsh censurer<br>严厉的谴责者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was the loudest censurer of the mayor's reckless spending.  他是对市长胡乱开支谴责得最响的人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 critic</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>批评者；评论家</td><td>a fierce critic<br>激烈的批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics of the plan warned about its enormous cost.  该计划的批评者警告其成本巨大。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 disparager</del></td><td>/dɪˈspærɪdʒər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>贬低者，毁谤者</td><td>a bitter disparager<br>刻薄的贬低者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called him a disparager of every new artistic movement.  批评者称他是所有新艺术运动的贬低者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 hypercritic</del></td><td>/ˌhaɪpərˈkrɪtɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>过分挑剔的人</td><td>a tireless hypercritic<br>不知疲倦的苛评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a hypercritic would complain about such a fine performance.  只有过分挑剔的人才会抱怨这么出色的演出。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nitpicker</del></td><td>/ˈnɪtˌpɪkər/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>吹毛求疵的人</td><td>a hopeless nitpicker<br>无可救药的吹毛求疵者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor was a nitpicker about every stray comma.  这位编辑对每个多余的逗号都吹毛求疵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>favorable</b></td><td>/ˈfeɪvərəbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利的</td><td>favorable conditions<br>有利的条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Favorable weather allowed the harvest to finish a week early.  有利的天气让收割提前一周完成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advantageous</td><td>/ˌædvənˈteɪdʒəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利的，有益的</td><td>an advantageous position<br>有利的位置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax rules proved advantageous to small local businesses.  新税收规定对本地小企业很有利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 benefic</td><td>/bəˈnɛfɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益的，有利的</td><td>a benefic influence<br>有益的影响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The reform had a benefic effect on rural schools.  这项改革对农村学校产生了有益影响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beneficial</td><td>/ˌbenəˈfɪʃəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益的，有利的</td><td>beneficial to your health<br>对健康有益</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Regular walking is beneficial to both your heart and mood.  经常步行对心脏和情绪都有益。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 favoring</td><td>/ˈfeɪvərɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利的，赞同的</td><td>a favoring wind<br>顺风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A favoring wind carried the small boats safely into harbor.  顺风把小船安全送进港口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 good</td><td>/ˈɡʊd/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有利的，好的</td><td>a good opportunity<br>有利的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This is a good moment to renegotiate the contract.  这是重新谈判合同的有利时机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 helpful</td><td>/ˈhelpfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有帮助的；乐于助人的</td><td>a helpful suggestion<br>有益的建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Your notes were extremely helpful when I revised the report.  我修改报告时你的笔记非常有帮助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 salutary</td><td>/ˈsæljəteri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益健康的；有利的，利好的</td><td>a salutary lesson<br>有益的教训</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The failed launch had a salutary effect on the careless habits of the team.  那次发射失败对团队的马虎习惯起了有益的作用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unfavorable</td><td>/ʌnˈfeɪvərəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不利的，不赞成的</td><td>unfavorable conditions<br>不利的条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unfavorable weather delayed the launch by two full days.  不利的天气使发射延迟了整整两天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 disadvantageous</td><td>/ˌdɪsædvənˈteɪdʒəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不利的，有害的</td><td>a disadvantageous position<br>不利的处境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax is disadvantageous to small family farms.  新税对小型家庭农场不利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 untoward</td><td>/ʌnˈtɔːrd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不幸的；难以驯服的，难以驾驭的</td><td>an untoward incident<br>意外的不幸事件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing untoward happened during the long flight across the ocean.  在这趟跨洋长途飞行中没有发生任何不幸的意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unpropitious</del></td><td>/ˌʌnprəˈpɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不吉利的，不利的</td><td>an unpropitious start<br>不利的开端</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project began under distinctly unpropitious circumstances.  该项目在明显不利的情况下启动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fawn</b></td><td>/fɔːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>阿谀奉承</td><td>fawn on the boss<br>讨好老板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Junior staff fawn on the boss whenever the director visits.  每当董事来访，下级员工就对老板阿谀奉承。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cower</td><td>/ˈkaʊər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>畏缩</td><td>cower in the corner<br>在角落里畏缩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The frightened puppy cowered under the table during the loud thunderstorm.  雷雨大作时，惊恐的小狗畏缩在桌子下面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cringe</td><td>/ˈkrɪndʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>卑躬屈膝；畏缩</td><td>cringe before the boss<br>在老板面前卑躬屈膝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The servant cringed whenever the duke raised his voice.  公爵一提高声音，仆人就畏缩起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flatter</td><td>/ˈflætər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>奉承；讨好；谄媚</td><td>flatter the boss<br>奉承老板</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He flattered the boss all morning hoping for a promotion.  他整个上午奉承老板，希望得到升职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grovel</td><td>/ˈɡrɑːvl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>卑躬屈膝</td><td>grovel before authority<br>在权威前卑躬屈膝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He refused to grovel before the committee just to keep his post.  他拒绝为了保住职位而在委员会面前卑躬屈膝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 toady</td><td>/ˈtoʊdi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>马屁精；拍马屁</td><td>toady to the powerful<br>对权贵拍马屁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Junior officials toady to the powerful hoping for quick promotion.  下级官员对权贵拍马屁，希望快速升迁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 truckle</del></td><td>/ˈtrʌkəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>屈从，谄媚</td><td>truckle to authority<br>屈从于权威</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He refused to truckle to the editors who paid him.  他拒绝向付钱给他的编辑们谄媚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 domineer</del></td><td>/ˌdɑːməˈnɪr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>专横跋扈，作威作福</td><td>domineer over his staff<br>专横地对待下属</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He liked to domineer over his staff instead of listening to their advice.  他喜欢专横地对待下属，而不是听取他们的建议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>faze</b></td><td>/feɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使慌乱，使窘迫</td><td>nothing seems to faze him<br>似乎没什么能扰乱他</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The huge crowd did not faze the young pianist at all.  庞大的观众丝毫没让这位年轻钢琴家慌乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 abash</del></td><td>/əˈbæʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬，使羞愧</td><td>be abashed by praise<br>因受赞扬而羞怯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked abashed when everyone applauded his clumsy speech.  当大家为他笨拙的演讲鼓掌时，他显得很窘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 annoy</td><td>/əˈnɔɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，不断烦扰</td><td>annoy the neighbors<br>不断打扰邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The barking dog began to annoy every neighbor on the quiet street.  那只不停犬吠的狗开始烦扰这条安静街上的每位邻居。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bother</td><td>/ˈbɑːðər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>打扰，使烦恼；费心</td><td>don't bother me<br>别烦我</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The constant noise from the street really bothers me.  街上不停的噪音真让我烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disconcert</td><td>/ˌdɪskənˈsɜːrt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不安，使慌乱</td><td>disconcert the audience<br>使观众不安</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His long silences disconcerted everyone at the table.  他长时间的沉默让餐桌上所有人都不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dismay</td><td>/dɪsˈmeɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使失去勇气；使不安，使焦虑</td><td>dismay his supporters<br>使支持者沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The abrupt policy reversal dismayed his supporters and delighted his rivals.  政策的突然反转让他的支持者沮丧，让对手高兴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disturb</td><td>/dɪˈstɜːrb/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打扰，扰乱；使不安</td><td>disturb the peace<br>扰乱安宁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please do not disturb the birds while they are nesting.  鸟类筑巢时请不要打扰它们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 discomfit</del></td><td>/dɪsˈkʌmfɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬；阻碍</td><td>discomfit an opponent<br>使对手窘迫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The blunt question visibly discomfited the confident speaker.  这个直率的问题明显让自信的演讲者局促不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 embarrass</td><td>/ɪmˈbærəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使尴尬</td><td>embarrass him in public<br>在公众面前使他尴尬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to embarrass her in public and only embarrassed himself.  他想在公众面前让她尴尬，结果只是让自己出丑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fluster</td><td>/ˈflʌstər/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使慌乱，使紧张</td><td>get flustered easily<br>容易慌乱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unexpected questions always fluster him during interviews.  面试中意外的问题总让他慌乱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rattle</td><td>/ˈrætəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>发出咔嗒声；使慌乱</td><td>rattle the windows<br>把窗户震得作响</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The passing train rattled every window in the old house.  驶过的火车震得老屋的每扇窗都咔咔作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lull</td><td>/lʌl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>相对平静时期，间隙；使镇静，使安心</td><td>a lull in the fighting<br>战斗中的间隙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brief lull in the fighting allowed medics to reach the wounded.  战斗中短暂的间隙让医护人员得以接近伤员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>feckless</b></td><td>/ˈfekləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无成果的，没有价值的；粗心不负责任的</td><td>a feckless young man<br>不成事的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His feckless handling of the accounts nearly ruined the shop.  他对账目粗心不负责的处理几乎毁了这家店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bootless</del></td><td>/ˈbuːtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无用的</td><td>a bootless effort<br>无用的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arguing with him about money proved a bootless effort.  和他争论钱的事结果是无用的努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fruitless</td><td>/ˈfruːtləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无成效的，白费的</td><td>a fruitless search<br>毫无结果的搜寻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks of fruitless negotiation left both sides exhausted.  数周毫无结果的谈判让双方都疲惫不堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 futile</td><td>/ˈfjuːtl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无效的，无用的</td><td>a futile attempt<br>徒劳的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trying to persuade him was clearly a futile attempt.  试图说服他显然是徒劳之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meaningless</td><td>/ˈmiːnɪŋləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无意义的，无价值的</td><td>a meaningless gesture<br>毫无意义的姿态</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Without any context these numbers are completely meaningless.  没有任何背景，这些数字毫无意义。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 purposeless</td><td>/ˈpɜːrpəsləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无目的的，漫无目标的</td><td>purposeless wandering<br>漫无目的的游荡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His purposeless days gradually blurred into one another.  他漫无目的的日子渐渐彼此混成一片。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 useless</td><td>/ˈjuːsləs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无用的，无效的</td><td>practically useless<br>几乎毫无用处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Without a charger the expensive phone was completely useless.  没有充电器，这部贵手机完全没用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 worthless</td><td>/ˈwɜːrθləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无价值的，没用的</td><td>a worthless promise<br>毫无价值的承诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old bonds proved worthless after the bank collapsed.  银行倒闭后那些旧债券变得一文不值。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 careless</td><td>/ˈkerləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，疏忽的</td><td>a careless mistake<br>粗心的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark ruined months of patient negotiation.  一句不慎的话毁了数月的耐心谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heedless</td><td>/ˈhiːdləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不注意的，不顾的</td><td>heedless of danger<br>不顾危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He ran into the street heedless of the traffic.  他不顾车流冲进了街道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inadvertent</td><td>/ˌɪnədˈvɜːrtənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非故意的，疏忽的</td><td>an inadvertent error<br>无意间的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An inadvertent keystroke deleted an entire afternoon of careful work.  一次无意的按键删掉了整个下午的细致工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irresponsible</td><td>/ˌɪrəˈspɑːnsəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不负责任的</td><td>irresponsible behavior<br>不负责任的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was irresponsible to leave the children entirely unsupervised.  让孩子完全无人看管是不负责任的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slipshod</td><td>/ˈslɪpʃɑːd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，随意的</td><td>slipshod workmanship<br>马虎的做工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The inspector criticized the slipshod workmanship on the newly built bridge.  检查员批评新建桥梁马虎的做工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sloppy</td><td>/ˈslɑːpi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，不整洁的；混乱的</td><td>sloppy handwriting<br>潦草的字迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sloppy handwriting made the exam answers almost impossible to read.  他潦草的字迹让考卷答案几乎无法辨认。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 effective</td><td>/ɪˈfektɪv/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有效的，起作用的</td><td>an effective solution<br>有效的解决办法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple hand washing is an effective way to prevent illness.  简单洗手是预防疾病的有效方法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 effectual</td><td>/ɪˈfɛktʃuəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有效的，起作用的</td><td>an effectual remedy<br>有效的疗法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple hand washing proved the most effectual precaution available.  简单的洗手被证明是最有效的预防措施。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 efficacious</td><td>/ˌefɪˈkeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有效的</td><td>an efficacious treatment<br>有效的治疗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old remedy proved surprisingly efficacious against mild seasonal coughs.  这个老方子治轻微的季节性咳嗽出乎意料地有效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 careful</td><td>/ˈkerfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，仔细的</td><td>careful consideration<br>慎重考虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Be careful when you cross this busy street at night.  夜里穿过这条繁忙的街道时要小心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cautious</td><td>/ˈkɔːʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>a cautious approach<br>谨慎的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Investors remained cautious until the election results were announced.  在选举结果公布前投资者一直很谨慎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 circumspect</td><td>/ˈsɜːrkəmspekt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，小心的</td><td>remain circumspect in public<br>在公开场合保持谨慎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Diplomats stay circumspect in public and blunt behind closed doors.  外交官在公开场合谨慎，关起门来则直言不讳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 discreet</td><td>/dɪˈskriːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（言行）谨慎的</td><td>a discreet inquiry<br>谨慎的询问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A discreet inquiry into his background turned up nothing worth reporting.  对他背景的谨慎查访没查出任何值得上报的事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wary</td><td>/ˈweri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>警惕的，谨慎的</td><td>wary of strangers<br>对陌生人警惕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children are taught to be wary of strangers offering free rides.  孩子们被教导要警惕主动提供搭车的陌生人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fecund</b></td><td>/ˈfiːkənd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多产的，肥沃的</td><td>a fecund imagination<br>丰富的想象力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novelist had a fecund imagination and published a book yearly.  这位小说家想象力丰富多产，每年都出一本书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cornucopian</del></td><td>/ˌkɔrnəˈkoʊpiən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>丰富的，充裕的</td><td>cornucopian abundance<br>极为丰饶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The market offered a cornucopian display of fruit.  市场上摆着丰富多样的水果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fertile</td><td>/ˈfɜːrtəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>肥沃的；多产的</td><td>fertile soil<br>肥沃的土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The valley's fertile soil supports three harvests each year.  河谷肥沃的土壤每年可支撑三次收成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fruitful</td><td>/ˈfruːtfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>富有成果的，多产的</td><td>a fruitful discussion<br>富有成果的讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their collaboration proved fruitful and produced several important papers.  他们的合作富有成果，产出了几篇重要论文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lush</td><td>/lʌʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>茂盛的，繁茂的</td><td>lush vegetation<br>茂盛的植被</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lush vegetation covered the valley floor after weeks of steady rain.  连续数周的降雨之后，茂盛的植被覆盖了谷底。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luxuriant</td><td>/lʌɡˈʒʊriənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>茂盛的，繁茂的</td><td>luxuriant vegetation<br>茂盛的植被</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Luxuriant ferns covered the damp floor of the forest.  茂盛的蕨类覆盖了潮湿的林地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 productive</td><td>/prəˈdʌktɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多产的，富有成效的</td><td>a productive meeting<br>富有成效的会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The morning was productive; we finished three chapters before lunch.  上午很有成效，午饭前我们写完了三章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prolific</td><td>/prəˈlɪfɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多产的，大量的</td><td>a prolific writer<br>多产的作家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a prolific painter who finished hundreds of canvases.  她是位多产的画家，完成了数百幅作品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rich</td><td>/ˈrɪtʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>肥沃的，丰富的；富有的</td><td>rich farmland<br>肥沃的农田</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The delta has rich soil ideal for growing rice.  三角洲土壤肥沃，非常适合种稻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 barren</td><td>/ˈbærən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无成果的</td><td>barren land<br>贫瘠的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing grows on the barren land beyond the last village.  最后一个村子之外的贫瘠土地上什么都长不出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infertile</td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrtəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；不育的</td><td>infertile ground<br>贫瘠的土地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Years of drought left the ground almost completely infertile.  多年干旱使土地几乎完全贫瘠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sterile</td><td>/ˈsterəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>贫瘠的；无菌的</td><td>sterile medical instruments<br>无菌医疗器械</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Surgeons must use sterile instruments to prevent infection during any operation.  外科医生必须使用无菌器械，以防手术中感染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>feeble</b></td><td>/ˈfiːbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>衰弱的</td><td>a feeble voice<br>微弱的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man answered in a feeble voice from the bed.  老人从床上用微弱的声音回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 debilitated</td><td>/dəˈbɪləˌteɪtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的，衰弱的</td><td>debilitated by long illness<br>因久病而衰弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The patient looked pale and debilitated after months of treatment.  经过数月治疗，病人显得苍白虚弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 effete</td><td>/ɪˈfiːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>衰弱的，衰落的</td><td>an effete aristocracy<br>衰落的贵族阶层</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An effete aristocracy clung to its titles long after the wealth had vanished.  衰落的贵族阶层在财富早已消失后仍死守头衔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enervated</td><td>/ˈɛnərveɪtəd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的，无力的</td><td>enervated by the heat<br>被暑热弄得虚弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long illness left him enervated and unusually pale.  长期患病使他虚弱且异常苍白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 faint</td><td>/ˈfeɪnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的；微弱的</td><td>a faint voice<br>微弱的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt faint after standing in the sun too long.  在太阳下站太久后她感到虚弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fragile</td><td>/ˈfrædʒl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易碎的</td><td>a fragile vase<br>易碎的花瓶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please pack the fragile vase in plenty of soft paper.  请用足够多的软纸把这个易碎的花瓶包好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frail</td><td>/freɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的；脆弱的</td><td>a frail old woman<br>体弱的老妇人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A frail old woman waited alone at the crowded bus stop.  一位虚弱的老妇人独自等在拥挤的公交站。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infirm</td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的；不果断的，优柔寡断的</td><td>old and infirm<br>年老体弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her grandmother grew too infirm to climb the stairs without help.  她的祖母身体太虚弱，没人帮忙就爬不了楼梯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 languid</td><td>/ˈlæŋɡwɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没精打采的，虚弱的</td><td>a languid summer afternoon<br>慢悠悠的夏日午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A languid summer afternoon passed with nobody willing to leave the shade.  一个慢悠悠的夏日午后过去了，没人愿意离开树荫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sapped</td><td>/ˈsæpt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>力气被耗尽的，虚弱的</td><td>sapped of energy<br>精力被耗尽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team looked sapped after three games in one week.  一周三场比赛后球队看来精力耗尽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mighty</td><td>/ˈmaɪti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强大的，有力的</td><td>a mighty river<br>浩荡的大河</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A mighty oak had stood there for three centuries.  一棵巨大的橡树在那里矗立了三个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 powerful</td><td>/ˈpaʊərfəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强大的，有力的</td><td>a powerful engine<br>强劲的发动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The telescope is powerful enough to show distant galaxies.  这台望远镜足够强大，能显示遥远的星系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 robust</td><td>/roʊˈbʌst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精力充沛的；强壮的，健康的</td><td>robust good health<br>强健的好身体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At eighty he remains robust enough to work in the garden daily.  他八十岁了，身体依然强健，每天还能在园中劳作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stalwart</td><td>/ˈstɔːlwərt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定忠诚的；强壮的</td><td>a stalwart supporter<br>坚定的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She remained a stalwart supporter of the project through every setback.  历经挫折，她始终是这个项目的坚定支持者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stout</td><td>/ˈstaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的，勇敢的；粗壮的</td><td>stout resistance<br>顽强的抵抗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The townspeople put up a stout defense against the raiders.  镇民对袭击者进行了顽强的抵抗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 strong</td><td>/strɔːŋ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>强壮的，坚固的</td><td>strong arms<br>强壮的臂膀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge is strong enough to carry heavy trucks.  这座桥足够坚固，能承载重型卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>feign</b></td><td>/feɪn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>制造假象，装出…的样子</td><td>feign illness<br>装病</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He feigned surprise when they announced the award.  他们宣布获奖时他装出惊讶的样子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affect</td><td>/əˈfekt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>假装，装作；影响</td><td>affect indifference<br>装作漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She affected surprise, though she had known for weeks.  她装作惊讶，其实早已知情数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assume</td><td>/əˈsuːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>就任，承担（职权）；假定</td><td>assume office<br>就职上任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She will assume office as director next Monday.  她将于下周一就任主任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 counterfeit</td><td>/ˈkaʊntərfɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>仿制的，假冒的；仿制</td><td>counterfeit banknotes<br>伪造的钞票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Police seized thousands of counterfeit banknotes hidden in the delivery van.  警方缴获了藏在送货车里的数千张假钞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissemble</td><td>/dɪˈsembl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>用假象隐藏真相，掩饰</td><td>dissemble his true motive<br>掩饰他的真实动机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He tried to dissemble his true motive, but his trembling hands betrayed him.  他试图掩饰真实动机，但颤抖的双手泄露了他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretend</td><td>/prɪˈtend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>假装，装作</td><td>pretend to be asleep<br>装睡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children pretended not to hear their mother calling.  孩子们假装没听见母亲呼唤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 profess</td><td>/prəˈfes/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>声称，假称</td><td>profess ignorance<br>声称不知情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He professed great sympathy but offered no actual help.  他声称非常同情，却没提供实际帮助。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sham</td><td>/ʃæm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>掩饰，假装；虚假的</td><td>a sham marriage<br>假婚姻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The couple admitted their marriage was a sham arranged for the visa.  这对夫妻承认他们的婚姻是为签证安排的假婚姻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 simulate</td><td>/ˈsɪmjuleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>模拟，假装</td><td>simulate battle conditions<br>模拟作战环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The training program can simulate battle conditions inside a quiet classroom.  该训练项目能在安静的教室里模拟作战环境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>foreshadow</b></td><td>/fɔːrˈʃædoʊ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预示</td><td>foreshadow a crisis<br>预示危机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The opening chapter foreshadows the crisis that destroys the family.  开篇一章预示了摧毁这个家庭的危机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 adumbrate</del></td><td>/ˈædəmbreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预示</td><td>adumbrate future trouble<br>预示日后的麻烦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The early chapters adumbrate the tragedy that follows.  开头几章预示了随后的悲剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 augur</td><td>/ˈɔːɡər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>预言家，占卜官；预言</td><td>augur well for the future<br>预示前景良好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The Roman augur studied the flight of birds before every battle.  这位罗马占卜官在每场战役前都观察鸟的飞行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 betoken</del></td><td>/bɪˈtoʊkən/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预示，表明</td><td>betoken trouble ahead<br>预示前方有麻烦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dark clouds on the horizon betokened a violent storm.  地平线上的乌云预示着一场猛烈风暴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 forebode</del></td><td>/fɔːrˈboʊd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>预示不祥；有预感</td><td>forebode coming trouble<br>预示麻烦将至</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden silence in the forest seemed to forebode coming trouble.  林中突然的寂静似乎预示麻烦将至。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 foreshow</del></td><td>/fɔrˈʃoʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预示，预兆</td><td>foreshow the outcome<br>预示结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These early results foreshow a difficult year for exporters.  这些早期结果预示出口商将有艰难的一年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 omen</td><td>/ˈoʊmən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>预兆，征兆</td><td>a bad omen<br>不好的预兆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sailors once read a blood-red sky as an omen.  水手曾把血红的天空视为预兆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 portend</td><td>/pɔːrˈtend/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预示，预兆</td><td>portend disaster<br>预示灾难</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Falling bond prices may portend a wider financial crisis.  债券价格下跌可能预示更广泛的金融危机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prefigure</td><td>/priːˈfɪɡjər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>预示，预先显示</td><td>prefigure later events<br>预示后来的事件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His early sketches prefigure the style of his mature paintings.  他早期的素描预示了他成熟画作的风格。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 presage</td><td>/ˈpresɪdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>征兆</td><td>presage of disaster<br>灾难的征兆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sudden silence of the birds was a presage of the storm.  鸟鸣骤然停止，是暴风雨来临的征兆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>feral</b></td><td>/ˈferəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>野生的</td><td>a feral cat<br>野猫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A feral cat had been living under the empty porch.  一只野猫一直住在空荡的门廊下面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 undomesticated</td><td>/ˌʌndəˈmɛstəkeɪtəd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未被驯化的，野生的</td><td>undomesticated animals<br>未驯化的动物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Undomesticated horses still roam parts of the western plains.  未驯化的马仍在西部平原部分地区游荡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 untamed</td><td>/ʌnˈteɪmd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未驯服的，野性的</td><td>untamed wilderness<br>未开化的荒野</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The island's untamed coastline attracts adventurous hikers every summer.  该岛未经驯化的海岸线每年夏天吸引爱冒险的徒步者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wild</td><td>/ˈwaɪld/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>荒唐的，毫无根据的；野生的</td><td>a wild guess<br>瞎猜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That's a wild guess with no evidence behind it.  那是毫无根据的胡乱猜测。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cultivated</td><td>/ˈkʌltɪveɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有修养的</td><td>a cultivated taste in art<br>高雅的艺术品位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her cultivated manners impressed everyone at the dinner.  她优雅的举止让晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fertilize</b></td><td>/ˈfɜːrtəlaɪz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使肥沃，使多产</td><td>fertilize the fields<br>给田地施肥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The farmers fertilize the fields twice before the rainy season.  农民在雨季前给田地施两次肥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enrich</td><td>/ɪnˈrɪtʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使丰富，增强</td><td>enrich the soil<br>使土壤肥沃</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reading widely will enrich your understanding of other cultures.  广泛阅读会丰富你对其他文化的理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 manure</td><td>/məˈnʊr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>肥料，粪肥；施肥</td><td>spread manure on the field<br>在田里撒粪肥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The farmer spread manure over the vegetable beds in spring.  农民春天在菜地上撒了粪肥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deplete</td><td>/dɪˈpliːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>耗尽，使衰竭</td><td>deplete natural resources<br>耗尽自然资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long drought depleted the town's water reserves.  长期干旱耗尽了小镇的储水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 drain</td><td>/ˈdreɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>耗尽，使枯竭</td><td>drain the country's resources<br>耗尽国家资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long war drained the country's resources and its morale.  漫长的战争耗尽了国家的资源和士气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exhaust</td><td>/ɪɡˈzɔːst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>耗尽</td><td>exhaust the water supply<br>耗尽水源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two dry summers exhausted the town's entire water supply.  连续两个干燥的夏天耗尽了全镇的水源。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fervid</b></td><td>/ˈfɜːrvɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的；热情的</td><td>a fervid supporter<br>热情的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His fervid speech moved even the quietest people in the room.  他热情洋溢的演讲连房间里最安静的人都被感动了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boiling</td><td>/ˈbɔɪlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沸腾的；情绪激昂的</td><td>a boiling pot of water<br>一锅沸水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She dropped the noodles into the boiling water and stirred once.  她把面条下进沸水里，搅了一下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hot</td><td>/ˈhɑːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>炎热的；辣的</td><td>a hot afternoon<br>炎热的午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was far too hot to work outside after noon.  午后太热，根本无法在户外工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scorching</td><td>/ˈskɔːrtʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的，灼热的</td><td>a scorching summer day<br>酷热的夏日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They crossed the desert under a scorching midday sun.  他们在酷热的正午阳光下穿越沙漠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 searing</td><td>/ˈsɪrɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>灼热的；尖锐的</td><td>searing heat<br>灼热的高温</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The searing heat of the furnace could be felt outside.  炉子的灼热在外面都能感觉到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sultry</td><td>/ˈsʌltri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>闷热的；性感撩人的</td><td>a sultry evening<br>闷热的夜晚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sultry air made sleeping almost impossible that night.  闷热的空气让那晚几乎无法入睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sweltering</td><td>/ˈsweltərɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的</td><td>a sweltering afternoon<br>酷热的午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We stayed indoors through the sweltering afternoon with the fans running.  酷热的午后我们开着风扇待在屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 torrid</td><td>/ˈtɔːrɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的；热情的，情感深厚的</td><td>the torrid zone<br>热带地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The torrid afternoon heat kept everyone indoors until the sun finally set.  酷热的午后让所有人待在室内，直到太阳终于落下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ardent</td><td>/ˈɑːrdənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，热切的</td><td>an ardent supporter<br>热情的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an ardent fan of the team for fifty years.  他五十年来一直是这支球队的狂热球迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fervent</td><td>/ˈfɜːrvənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满感情的，热情洋溢的</td><td>a fervent believer<br>热切的信徒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is a fervent believer in free public libraries for everyone.  她热切地相信应该为所有人提供免费的公共图书馆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impassioned</td><td>/ɪmˈpæʃənd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>慷慨激昂的，充满激情的</td><td>an impassioned speech<br>慷慨激昂的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave an impassioned defense of public libraries.  她慷慨激昂地为公共图书馆辩护。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 passionate</td><td>/ˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，充满激情的</td><td>a passionate defense of art<br>对艺术的热情辩护</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is passionate about protecting the city's old neighborhoods.  她对保护城市的老街区充满热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 perfervid</del></td><td>/pərˈfɜrvɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极热情的，狂热的</td><td>perfervid enthusiasm<br>狂热的热情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His perfervid loyalty to the team bordered on obsession.  他对球队狂热的忠诚近乎痴迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 arctic</td><td>/ˈɑːrktɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极寒的；北极的</td><td>arctic temperatures<br>极寒的气温</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An arctic wind swept across the empty parking lot.  一阵极寒的风扫过空荡的停车场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 chilling</td><td>/ˈtʃɪlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺骨的；令人恐惧的</td><td>a chilling wind<br>刺骨的寒风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A chilling drizzle soaked us before we reached shelter.  刺骨的细雨在我们到达庇护处前把我们淋透。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cold</td><td>/ˈkoʊld/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>寒冷的；冷淡的</td><td>a cold morning<br>寒冷的早晨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The water was much too cold for anyone to swim.  水太冷了，没人能游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 freezing</td><td>/ˈfriːzɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极冷的，冰冻的</td><td>freezing temperatures<br>冰点以下的气温</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We waited on the freezing platform for half an hour.  我们在极冷的站台上等了半小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 frigid</td><td>/ˈfrɪdʒɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严寒的；冷漠的</td><td>a frigid stare<br>冷冰冰的凝视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the latecomer a frigid stare and said nothing.  她冷冰冰地盯了迟到者一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 frozen</td><td>/ˈfroʊzən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冻结的，冰冻的</td><td>a frozen lake<br>结冰的湖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Children skated across the frozen pond behind the church.  孩子们在教堂后结冰的池塘上滑冰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glacial</td><td>/ˈɡleɪsiəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极冷的；冷漠的，无感情的</td><td>a glacial silence<br>冰冷的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His glacial tone made everyone at the table stop talking at once.  他冰冷的语气让桌边所有人立刻停止了交谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 icy</td><td>/ˈaɪsi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冰冷的；冷淡的</td><td>an icy road<br>结冰的道路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave him an icy stare and said nothing.  她冷冰冰地瞪了他一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cool</td><td>/ˈkuːl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>凉爽的；冷淡的</td><td>a cool reception<br>冷淡的接待</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His proposal met a cool response from the committee.  他的提案得到委员会冷淡的回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dispassionate</td><td>/dɪsˈpæʃənət/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静客观的，不带感情的</td><td>a dispassionate analysis<br>冷静客观的分析</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge gave a dispassionate summary of both arguments.  法官冷静客观地总结了双方的论点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 emotionless</td><td>/ɪˈmoʊʃənləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无感情的，冷漠的</td><td>an emotionless voice<br>毫无感情的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He read the verdict in a flat, emotionless tone.  他用平板毫无感情的语调宣读了判决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 impassive</td><td>/ɪmˈpæsɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷漠的，无感情的</td><td>remain impassive under pressure<br>压力下仍无动于衷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His face stayed impassive as the verdict was read aloud.  宣读判决时，他的脸上始终毫无表情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unemotional</td><td>/ˌʌnɪˈmoʊʃənəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不动感情的，冷静的</td><td>an unemotional account<br>不带感情的叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her unemotional summary of the disaster unsettled the listeners.  她不带感情的灾难总结让听众不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>forte</b></td><td>/fɔːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>优势，长处</td><td>negotiation is her forte<br>谈判是她的长处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Negotiation is her forte, so let her handle the meeting.  谈判是她的长处，让她来主持这次会议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 advantage</td><td>/ədˈvæntɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>优势，好处</td><td>a clear advantage<br>明显的优势</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Speaking three languages gave her a real advantage here.  会说三种语言在这里给了她真正的优势。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 eminency</del></td><td>/ˈɛmənənsi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>卓越，杰出</td><td>reach eminency in science<br>在科学上达到卓越</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He achieved eminency in a field few people understood.  他在少有人懂的领域达到了卓越。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 knack</td><td>/næk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>诀窍，聪明的做法</td><td>a knack for languages<br>学语言的天分</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My sister has a remarkable knack for learning foreign languages quickly.  我妹妹在快速学外语上有惊人的天分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 medium</td><td>/ˈmiːdiəm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>擅长的表现形式；媒介</td><td>her favorite medium<br>她最爱的表现媒介</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Watercolor is the medium in which he works best.  水彩是他最擅长的表现媒介。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 métier</td><td>/ˈmɛtjeɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>擅长的职业，本行</td><td>find one's métier<br>找到自己的本行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teaching turned out to be her true métier after all.  教书最终成了她真正擅长的本行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 oyster</td><td>/ˈɔɪstər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>可任意享用之物；牡蛎</td><td>the world is your oyster<br>世界任你闯荡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With a degree like that, the world is your oyster.  有那样的学位，世界任你闯荡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 talent</td><td>/ˈtælənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>才能，天赋</td><td>a talent for drawing<br>绘画的天赋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She showed an unusual talent for mathematics very early.  她很早就显出不凡的数学天赋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inadequacy</td><td>/ɪnˈædɪkwəsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不足，能力不够</td><td>a sense of inadequacy<br>能力不足感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report exposed the inadequacy of existing safety rules.  报告揭示了现有安全规定的不足。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incapability</td><td>/ˌɪnkeɪpəˈbɪləti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无能力，不能胜任</td><td>incapability of leadership<br>缺乏领导能力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His incapability became obvious during the very first crisis.  他的无能在第一次危机中就显露出来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incompetence</td><td>/ɪnˈkɑːmpətəns/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>无能，不胜任</td><td>sheer incompetence<br>十足的无能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long delays were blamed on administrative incompetence.  长时间的延误被归咎于行政上的无能。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inefficiency</td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈfɪʃənsi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>效率低下，无效</td><td>bureaucratic inefficiency<br>官僚的低效</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inefficiency in the supply chain doubled the delivery time.  供应链的低效使交货时间翻了一倍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fester</b></td><td>/ˈfestər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>感染，溃烂，腐烂</td><td>the wound festered badly<br>伤口严重溃烂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The untreated wound festered badly within only two days.  未经处理的伤口仅两天就严重溃烂了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 corrupt</td><td>/kəˈrʌpt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>腐败的；使腐化，收买</td><td>a corrupt official<br>一名腐败官员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The corrupt inspector accepted cash to ignore the violations.  这名腐败的检查员收现金来无视违规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decompose</td><td>/ˌdiːkəmˈpoʊz/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>腐烂，分解</td><td>decompose into soil<br>分解成土壤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fallen leaves decompose quickly in warm, wet weather.  落叶在温暖潮湿的天气里迅速腐烂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 putrefy</del></td><td>/ˈpjuːtrəfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>腐烂，腐败</td><td>meat begins to putrefy<br>肉开始腐烂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Left in the sun, the fish soon began to putrefy.  鱼在太阳下放着，很快开始腐烂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rot</td><td>/ˈrɑːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>腐烂，腐坏</td><td>rot in the damp<br>在潮湿中腐烂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wooden posts had rotted through at the base.  木桩底部已经烂穿了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spoil</td><td>/ˈspɔɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>损坏，破坏；溺爱</td><td>spoil the surprise<br>破坏惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy rain spoiled the picnic we had planned for weeks.  大雨破坏了我们筹划数周的野餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heal</td><td>/ˈhiːl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>治愈，痊愈</td><td>heal a wound<br>治愈伤口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The deep cut took nearly a month to heal completely.  那道深伤口花了近一个月才完全愈合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cure</td><td>/ˈkjʊr/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>治愈，治疗；疗法</td><td>cure the infection<br>治愈感染</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Antibiotics cured the infection within a single week.  抗生素在一周内治愈了感染。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fetid</b></td><td>/ˈfetɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的</td><td>fetid air<br>恶臭的空气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fetid smell rose from the blocked drain behind the house.  屋后堵塞的下水道散出恶臭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
