</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 robust</td><td>/roʊˈbʌst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精力充沛的；强壮的，健康的</td><td>robust good health<br>强健的好身体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At eighty he remains robust enough to work in the garden daily.  他八十岁了，身体依然强健，每天还能在园中劳作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 faithful</td><td>/ˈfeɪθfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忠实的；如实的</td><td>a faithful translation<br>忠实的译文</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film is a faithful adaptation of the original novel.  这部电影是对原著小说的忠实改编。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 resolute</td><td>/ˈrezəluːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的</td><td>remain resolute<br>保持坚定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite heavy criticism, the mayor remained resolute about closing the old factory.  尽管备受批评，市长在关闭旧工厂一事上依然坚定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 9

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fraudulent</b></td><td>/ˈfrɔːdʒələnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欺诈的</td><td>fraudulent claims<br>欺诈性的索赔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The insurer refused to pay several obviously fraudulent claims last year.  去年保险公司拒付了几笔明显欺诈的索赔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deceitful</td><td>/dəˈsiːtfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>欺诈的，骗人的</td><td>deceitful practices<br>欺诈的做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The firm was fined for deceitful advertising practices.  该公司因欺诈性广告做法被罚款。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deceptive</td><td>/dɪˈseptɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欺骗性的，误导的</td><td>deceptive advertising<br>欺骗性广告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The calm surface of the river is deceptive and dangerous.  河面的平静具有欺骗性，很危险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dishonest</td><td>/dɪˈsɑːnəst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不诚实的，欺骗的</td><td>a dishonest business practice<br>不诚实的商业做法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company used dishonest advertising to attract careless customers.  公司用不诚实的广告吸引不细心的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 duplicitous</td><td>/duːˈplɪsɪtəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>两面派的，奸诈的</td><td>duplicitous behavior<br>两面派的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His duplicitous behavior toward both partners eventually destroyed the company.  他对两位合伙人的两面派行为最终毁了公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 guileful</del></td><td>/ˈɡaɪlfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡诈的，欺骗的</td><td>a guileful negotiator<br>狡诈的谈判者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guileful merchant tricked several villagers out of their savings.  这个狡诈的商人骗走了几位村民的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 underhanded</td><td>/ˌʌndərˈhændɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>狡诈的，暗中进行的</td><td>underhanded tactics<br>暗中使用的手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They won the contract through underhanded tactics.  他们靠不正当手段赢得了合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reliable</td><td>/rɪˈlaɪəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可靠的，可信赖的</td><td>a reliable source<br>可靠的消息来源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The story came from a reliable source inside the ministry.  这条消息来自部内一个可靠的来源。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 trustworthy</td><td>/ˈtrʌstwɜːrði/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>值得信赖的，可靠的</td><td>a trustworthy friend<br>值得信赖的朋友</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He proved a trustworthy friend during her worst year.  在她最糟的一年里他证明是值得信赖的朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fraught</b></td><td>/frɔːt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满（危险）的；忧虑的</td><td>fraught with danger<br>充满危险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mountain route is fraught with danger in early spring.  早春时节这条山路危机四伏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abounding</td><td>/əˈbaʊndɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满的，大量的</td><td>abounding in fish<br>盛产鱼类</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They found a river abounding in trout.  他们发现一条鳟鱼很多的河。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abundant</td><td>/əˈbʌndənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，丰富的</td><td>abundant evidence<br>大量的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water was abundant after the spring rains.  春雨过后水源充足。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 awash</td><td>/əˈwɔːʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（如洪水般）泛滥的</td><td>awash with cash<br>现金泛滥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The small town was awash with cash during the oil boom.  石油繁荣时期，这个小镇资金充裕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flush</td><td>/flʌʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、不及物动词</td><td>充裕的，有钱的；冲洗</td><td>flush with money<br>钱很充裕</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After the bonus he was flush with money for months.  拿到奖金后他有好几个月钱都很充裕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 replete</td><td>/rɪˈpliːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满…的，富于…的</td><td>replete with errors<br>错误连篇</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His essay was replete with spelling errors.  他的文章满是拼写错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thronging</td><td>/ˈθrɑːŋɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>蜂拥而至的，拥挤的</td><td>thronging crowds<br>蜂拥的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thronging fans filled the stadium hours before the concert began.  蜂拥的粉丝在演唱会开始前几小时就挤满了体育场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scarce</td><td>/ˈskers/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>稀缺的，不足的</td><td>in scarce supply<br>供应短缺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fresh water became scarce during the drought.  旱灾期间淡水变得稀缺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>frenzy</b></td><td>/ˈfrenzi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狂乱；狂热；疯狂</td><td>in a frenzy of excitement<br>处于极度兴奋中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd worked itself into a frenzy when the singer finally appeared.  歌手终于出场时，人群陷入了一片狂热。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delirium</td><td>/dɪˈlɪriəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精神错乱；极度兴奋，发狂</td><td>in a feverish delirium<br>处于发烧的昏乱中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For two nights the fever kept him in a restless delirium.  高烧使他两夜都处在不安的精神错乱之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fever</td><td>/ˈfiːvər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂热，极度激动；发烧</td><td>election fever<br>选举狂热</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Election fever gripped the small town for weeks.  选举狂热笼罩了这个小镇数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 furor</td><td>/ˈfjʊrɔːr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，狂怒；激动，狂热</td><td>cause a public furor<br>引起公众哗然</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting caused a public furor when it was first shown.  这幅画首次展出时引起公众哗然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fury</td><td>/ˈfjʊri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>暴怒</td><td>fly into a fury<br>勃然大怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He flew into a fury when he saw the broken window.  他看到破碎的窗户时勃然大怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hysteria</td><td>/hɪˈsteriə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>歇斯底里，狂热</td><td>mass hysteria in the crowd<br>人群中的集体狂热</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rumors of invasion produced mass hysteria across the small town.  入侵的谣言在小镇引发集体歇斯底里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insanity</td><td>/ɪnˈsænəti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>精神失常，疯狂</td><td>a plea of insanity<br>精神失常的抗辩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lawyer entered a plea of insanity at trial.  他的律师在庭审时提出精神失常抗辩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rage</td><td>/reɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暴怒</td><td>fly into a rage<br>勃然大怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He flew into a rage when the waiter spilled the soup.  服务员把汤打翻时，他勃然大怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rampage</td><td>/ˈræmˌpeɪdʒ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>横冲直撞，暴怒乱闯</td><td>go on a rampage<br>大肆撒野闹事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Elephants rampaged through the village, destroying several huts.  大象横冲直撞穿过村子，毁了几间棚屋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uproar</td><td>/ˈʌˌprɔːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，骚动</td><td>cause an uproar<br>引起轩然大波</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax plan caused an uproar among small business owners.  新税收方案在小企业主中引起轩然大波。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sanity</td><td>/ˈsænəti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>心智健全，神志正常</td><td>question his sanity<br>质疑他的神志是否正常</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lawyers questioned his sanity at the time of the robbery.  他的律师质疑他在抢劫时神志是否正常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>farfetched</b></td><td>/ˌfɑːrˈfetʃt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>牵强的，不可信的</td><td>a farfetched explanation<br>牵强的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His farfetched explanation convinced nobody on the committee.  他牵强的解释没能说服委员会里任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 doubtful</td><td>/ˈdaʊtfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以确定的，可疑的</td><td>of doubtful value<br>价值可疑的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is doubtful whether the bridge can carry such loads.  这座桥能否承受如此重量还很难说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dubious</td><td>/ˈduːbiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>充满不定性的，值得怀疑的</td><td>a dubious claim<br>可疑的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The advertisement made a dubious claim that no serious scientist would support.  那则广告作出了一个没有严肃科学家会支持的可疑说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 questionable</td><td>/ˈkwestʃənəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可疑的，靠不住的</td><td>questionable evidence<br>可疑的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The deal rested on questionable accounting practices.  这笔交易建立在可疑的会计做法上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unapt</del></td><td>/ʌnˈæpt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不恰当的；不易于……的</td><td>an unapt comparison<br>不恰当的比较</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That is an unapt comparison between two very different cases.  那是对两个截然不同案例的不恰当比较。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unlikely</td><td>/ʌnˈlaɪkli/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不可能的，未必的</td><td>an unlikely story<br>不可信的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It seems unlikely that the plan will be approved.  这个计划看来不太可能获批。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fretful</b></td><td>/ˈfretfl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，烦躁的</td><td>a fretful child<br>烦躁的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The baby was fretful all night because of the heat.  因为炎热，婴儿整夜烦躁不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irritable</td><td>/ˈɪrətəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，急躁的</td><td>become irritable and tense<br>变得易怒紧张</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lack of sleep makes him irritable by mid-afternoon.  睡眠不足使他到下午中段就变得易怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fractious</td><td>/ˈfrækʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以管束的</td><td>a fractious child<br>难管束的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The fractious child refused to sit still during the whole flight.  这个难以管束的孩子整趟飞行都不肯安坐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peevish</td><td>/ˈpiːvɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，爱抱怨的</td><td>a peevish complaint<br>不耐烦的抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She answered in a peevish tone and refused to explain further.  她用不耐烦的语气回答，拒绝再解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 pettish</del></td><td>/ˈpɛtɪʃ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，任性烦躁的</td><td>a pettish reply<br>烦躁任性的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave a pettish reply and slammed the door.  她烦躁地回了一句，然后摔上了门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 petulant</td><td>/ˈpetʃələnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易怒的，爱发脾气的</td><td>a petulant remark<br>使性子的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The actor made a petulant remark and stormed off the stage.  那位演员使性子说了句话，然后气冲冲地离开了舞台。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 easygoing</td><td>/ˌiːziˈɡoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>随和的，脾气好的</td><td>an easygoing manner<br>随和的态度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her easygoing nature makes her popular with colleagues.  她随和的性格使她受同事欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>friable</b></td><td>/ˈfraɪəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易碎的</td><td>friable dry soil<br>易碎的干土</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The friable dry soil crumbled easily between his rough fingers.  易碎的干土在他粗糙的手指间轻易碎裂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crumbly</td><td>/ˈkrʌmbli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>易碎成屑的，松散的</td><td>crumbly cheese<br>易碎的奶酪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soil here is dry and crumbly between the fingers.  这里的土干燥松散，一捏就散。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delicate</td><td>/ˈdeləkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，精细的；脆弱的</td><td>a delicate distinction<br>微妙的区别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a delicate ear for the subtle differences between dialects.  她对方言之间的细微差别有很灵敏的耳朵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 feeble</td><td>/ˈfiːbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>衰弱的</td><td>a feeble voice<br>微弱的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man answered in a feeble voice from the bed.  老人从床上用微弱的声音回答。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fragile</td><td>/ˈfrædʒl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>易碎的</td><td>a fragile vase<br>易碎的花瓶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please pack the fragile vase in plenty of soft paper.  请用足够多的软纸把这个易碎的花瓶包好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frail</td><td>/freɪl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的；脆弱的</td><td>a frail old woman<br>体弱的老妇人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A frail old woman waited alone at the crowded bus stop.  一位虚弱的老妇人独自等在拥挤的公交站。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 infirm</td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的；不果断的，优柔寡断的</td><td>old and infirm<br>年老体弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her grandmother grew too infirm to climb the stairs without help.  她的祖母身体太虚弱，没人帮忙就爬不了楼梯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weak</td><td>/ˈwiːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>脆弱的，虚弱的</td><td>a weak structure<br>脆弱的结构</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bridge has a weak structure and cannot bear trucks.  这座桥结构脆弱，无法承载卡车。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sturdy</td><td>/ˈstɜːrdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强健的，结实的</td><td>a sturdy wooden table<br>结实的木桌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sturdy wooden table stood in the middle of the farmhouse kitchen.  农舍厨房中间摆着一张结实的木桌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 substantial</td><td>/səbˈstænʃl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>大量的，可观的；实质的</td><td>a substantial increase<br>大幅的增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company reported a substantial increase in profits this quarter.  公司报告本季度利润大幅增长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>friction</b></td><td>/ˈfrɪkʃn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>摩擦；冲突，不和</td><td>friction between them<br>他们之间的摩擦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant friction between the two managers slowed the whole project.  两位经理之间不断的摩擦拖慢了整个项目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abrasion</td><td>/əˈbreɪʒən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>磨损，擦伤</td><td>resistant to abrasion<br>耐磨损</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This coating is highly resistant to abrasion and heat.  这种涂层极耐磨损和高温。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disagreement</td><td>/dɪsəˈɡriːmənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分歧，不一致，争执</td><td>a sharp disagreement<br>激烈的分歧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A long disagreement over funding stalled the whole project.  关于资金的长期分歧使整个项目停滞。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conflict</td><td>/ˈkɑːnflɪkt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>冲突，矛盾；抵触</td><td>a conflict of interest<br>利益冲突</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge withdrew because of a possible conflict of interest.  法官因可能存在利益冲突而回避。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contention</td><td>/kənˈtenʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>争执，争论；论点</td><td>a point of contention<br>争议之点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water rights remain a point of contention between the villages.  水权仍是各村之间的争议之点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dissidence</td><td>/ˈdɪsədəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>异议，不同意</td><td>political dissidence<br>政治异见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The regime punished even mild dissidence among its writers.  该政权连作家中温和的异见也要惩罚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 schism</td><td>/ˈskɪzəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>分裂，派系对立</td><td>a bitter schism<br>严重的分裂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bitter schism split the party into two hostile factions.  一场激烈的分裂把该党撕成两个敌对派系。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 war</td><td>/ˈwɔːr/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>冲突，斗争；战争</td><td>a price war<br>价格战</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A price war between the two airlines cut fares sharply.  两家航空公司的价格战大幅压低了票价。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 accord</td><td>/əˈkɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>一致，符合；授予</td><td>in accord with<br>与…一致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His actions were in accord with the policy he announced.  他的行动与他宣布的政策一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 harmony</td><td>/ˈhɑːrməni/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和谐，融洽</td><td>live in harmony with<br>与…和谐共处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two communities have lived in harmony for generations.  这两个社区世代和睦相处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peace</td><td>/ˈpiːs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>和平，和睦；平静</td><td>live in peace<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two villages have lived in peace for over a century.  这两个村庄和睦相处已逾一个世纪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concord</td><td>/ˈkɑːŋkɔːrd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>一致，和睦</td><td>live in concord<br>和睦相处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The treaty brought decades of concord between the two states.  该条约给两国带来数十年的和睦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>frigid</b></td><td>/ˈfrɪdʒɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严寒的；冷漠的</td><td>a frigid stare<br>冷冰冰的凝视</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the latecomer a frigid stare and said nothing.  她冷冰冰地盯了迟到者一眼，什么也没说。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cold</td><td>/ˈkoʊld/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>寒冷的；冷淡的</td><td>a cold morning<br>寒冷的早晨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The water was much too cold for anyone to swim.  水太冷了，没人能游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arctic</td><td>/ˈɑːrktɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极寒的；北极的</td><td>arctic temperatures<br>极寒的气温</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An arctic wind swept across the empty parking lot.  一阵极寒的风扫过空荡的停车场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chilling</td><td>/ˈtʃɪlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>刺骨的；令人恐惧的</td><td>a chilling wind<br>刺骨的寒风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A chilling drizzle soaked us before we reached shelter.  刺骨的细雨在我们到达庇护处前把我们淋透。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 freezing</td><td>/ˈfriːzɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极冷的，冰冻的</td><td>freezing temperatures<br>冰点以下的气温</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We waited on the freezing platform for half an hour.  我们在极冷的站台上等了半小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frosty</td><td>/ˈfrɔːsti/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>寒冷的；无强烈感情的，冷淡的</td><td>a frosty welcome<br>冷淡的欢迎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The visitors received a frosty welcome from the village elders.  来访者受到村中长者冷淡的接待。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glacial</td><td>/ˈɡleɪsiəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极冷的；冷漠的，无感情的</td><td>a glacial silence<br>冰冷的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His glacial tone made everyone at the table stop talking at once.  他冰冷的语气让桌边所有人立刻停止了交谈。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indifferent</td><td>/ɪnˈdɪfrənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不感兴趣的，冷漠的；平庸的</td><td>indifferent to criticism<br>对批评漠不关心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is entirely indifferent to what her critics say.  她对批评者说什么完全无所谓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 emotionless</td><td>/ɪˈmoʊʃənləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无感情的，冷漠的</td><td>an emotionless voice<br>毫无感情的声音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He read the verdict in a flat, emotionless tone.  他用平板毫无感情的语调宣读了判决。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 passionless</td><td>/ˈpæʃənləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷淡的，无激情的</td><td>a passionless performance<br>毫无激情的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic dismissed it as a technically perfect but passionless performance.  评论家称之为技术完美却毫无激情的表演。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unresponsive</td><td>/ˌʌnrɪˈspɑːnsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷淡的，无反应的</td><td>an unresponsive audience<br>毫无反应的观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The comedian struggled before a cold, unresponsive audience.  这位喜剧演员面对冷淡无反应的观众很吃力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 roasting</td><td>/ˈroʊstɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的，灼热的</td><td>a roasting afternoon<br>酷热的下午</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We crossed the desert on a roasting July afternoon.  我们在七月一个酷热的下午穿过沙漠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scalding</td><td>/ˈskɔːldɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>滚烫的；（言辞）严厉的</td><td>scalding criticism<br>严厉的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The critic wrote a scalding review of the opening night.  评论家为首演之夜写了一篇严厉的评论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scorching</td><td>/ˈskɔːrtʃɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的，灼热的</td><td>a scorching summer day<br>酷热的夏日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They crossed the desert under a scorching midday sun.  他们在酷热的正午阳光下穿越沙漠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 searing</td><td>/ˈsɪrɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>灼热的；尖锐的</td><td>searing heat<br>灼热的高温</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The searing heat of the furnace could be felt outside.  炉子的灼热在外面都能感觉到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 seething</td><td>/ˈsiːðɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沸腾般的；极为愤怒的</td><td>seething with anger<br>怒火中烧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sat silently, seething with anger at the insult.  他沉默地坐着，为这侮辱怒火中烧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sizzling</td><td>/ˈsɪzəlɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>灼热的；热烈的</td><td>sizzling heat<br>灼人的酷热</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sizzling heat kept everyone indoors until evening.  灼人的酷热让所有人待在室内直到傍晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sultry</td><td>/ˈsʌltri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>闷热的；性感撩人的</td><td>a sultry evening<br>闷热的夜晚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sultry air made sleeping almost impossible that night.  闷热的空气让那晚几乎无法入睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sweltering</td><td>/ˈsweltərɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的</td><td>a sweltering afternoon<br>酷热的午后</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We stayed indoors through the sweltering afternoon with the fans running.  酷热的午后我们开着风扇待在屋里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 torrid</td><td>/ˈtɔːrɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>酷热的；热情的，情感深厚的</td><td>the torrid zone<br>热带地区</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The torrid afternoon heat kept everyone indoors until the sun finally set.  酷热的午后让所有人待在室内，直到太阳终于落下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ardent</td><td>/ˈɑːrdənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情的，热切的</td><td>an ardent supporter<br>热情的支持者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He remained an ardent fan of the team for fifty years.  他五十年来一直是这支球队的狂热球迷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 amorous</td><td>/ˈæmərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>多情的，充满爱意的</td><td>amorous glances<br>含情的目光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sent her amorous letters throughout that long summer.  整个漫长的夏天他都给她写情意绵绵的信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 cordial</td><td>/ˈkɔːrdʒəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>热情友好的；令人振奋的</td><td>a cordial welcome<br>热情的欢迎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two leaders exchanged cordial greetings before the talks.  两位领导人在会谈前互致亲切的问候。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fringe</b></td><td>/frɪndʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>边缘</td><td>on the fringe of society<br>处于社会边缘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel describes families living on the fringe of society.  这部小说描写生活在社会边缘的家庭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 border</td><td>/ˈbɔːrdər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>边界，边缘</td><td>cross the border<br>越过边界</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They crossed the border shortly before dawn.  他们在黎明前不久越过了边界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 boundary</td><td>/ˈbaʊndəri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>边界，界限</td><td>the boundary between fields<br>田地之间的边界</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A low stone wall marks the boundary between the farms.  一道矮石墙标出两个农场的边界。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 circumference</td><td>/sərˈkʌmfərəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>圆周，周长</td><td>the circumference of the circle<br>圆的周长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Measure the circumference of the trunk at chest height.  在胸高处测量树干的周长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 edge</td><td>/ˈedʒ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>边缘，边沿</td><td>the edge of the cliff<br>悬崖边缘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stood at the edge of the cliff and looked down.  她站在悬崖边缘往下看。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 margin</td><td>/ˈmɑːrdʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>边缘；页边空白；差额</td><td>the margin of the lake<br>湖的边缘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tall reeds grow along the margin of the lake.  高高的芦苇沿着湖边生长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perimeter</td><td>/pəˈrɪmɪtər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>边界，界限</td><td>guard the perimeter<br>守卫外围边界</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soldiers guarded the perimeter of the camp throughout the night.  士兵们整夜守卫着营地的周边界线。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 periphery</td><td>/pəˈrɪfəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>外围，边缘</td><td>the periphery of the city<br>城市的外围</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 New factories sprang up on the periphery of the city.  新工厂在城市外围拔地而起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 skirt</td><td>/skɜːrt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>绕行，避开</td><td>skirt the issue<br>回避问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The minister tried to skirt the issue with a long, vague answer.  部长试图用冗长含糊的回答回避这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 center</td><td>/ˈsentər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>使集中；中心</td><td>center attention on quality<br>把注意力集中于质量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report centers its argument on three very clear examples.  报告把论点集中在三个非常清晰的例子上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 core</td><td>/ˈkɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，要点</td><td>the core of the argument<br>论点的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At the core of the dispute lies a simple money question.  争端的核心是一个简单的金钱问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>frivolous</b></td><td>/ˈfrɪvələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻浮的；无关紧要的</td><td>a frivolous remark<br>轻率无谓的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge dismissed the frivolous lawsuit within a single morning.  法官在一个上午之内就驳回了这起无理的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 giddy</td><td>/ˈɡɪdi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻率不严肃的</td><td>a giddy schoolgirl<br>轻率的女学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The giddy schoolgirls laughed through the entire serious lecture.  那些轻率的女学生在整场严肃的讲座中笑个不停。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 shallow</td><td>/ˈʃæloʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浅薄的，肤浅的；浅的</td><td>shallow water<br>浅水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His arguments are shallow and easy to refute.  他的论点浅薄，很容易反驳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 superficial</td><td>/ˌsuːpərˈfɪʃl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>表面的，肤浅的</td><td>a superficial understanding<br>肤浅的理解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has only a superficial knowledge of the subject.  她对这门学科只有肤浅的了解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flimsy</td><td>/ˈflɪmzi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>站不住脚的；不结实的</td><td>a flimsy excuse<br>站不住脚的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered a flimsy excuse for missing the meeting.  他为缺席会议给出了一个站不住脚的借口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 light</td><td>/ˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻微的，不严重的</td><td>a light meal<br>一顿便餐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He suffered only light injuries in the accident.  他在事故中只受了轻伤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 petty</td><td>/ˈpeti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>petty complaints<br>琐碎的抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He wasted the meeting on petty complaints about parking spaces.  他把会议浪费在关于停车位的琐碎抱怨上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trifling</td><td>/ˈtraɪflɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>细微的，不重要的</td><td>a trifling sum<br>微不足道的一笔钱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He dismissed the missing coins as a trifling sum not worth reporting.  他把丢失的硬币当作微不足道的小钱，不值得上报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 earnest</td><td>/ˈɜːrnɪst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严肃认真的</td><td>an earnest student<br>认真的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An earnest student stayed behind to ask about the summer reading list.  一名认真的学生留下来询问暑期阅读书目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sober</td><td>/ˈsoʊbər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严肃的；节制的</td><td>a sober assessment<br>冷静的评估</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee offered a sober assessment of the country's economic prospects.  委员会对国家经济前景作出冷静的评估。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 serious</td><td>/ˈsɪriəs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严肃的，认真的；严重的</td><td>a serious student<br>认真的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is serious about becoming a research scientist.  她认真地想成为一名研究科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 consequential</td><td>/ˌkɑːnsəˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；随之而来的</td><td>a consequential ruling<br>一项重要的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That quiet vote proved the most consequential act of the session.  那次不起眼的投票成了本届会议最重要的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 eventful</td><td>/ɪˈventfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>多事的，重要的</td><td>an eventful year<br>多事的一年</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It has been an eventful year for the whole family.  对全家来说这是多事的一年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 meaningful</td><td>/ˈmiːnɪŋfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有意义的，意味深长的</td><td>meaningful work<br>有意义的工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left banking to find more meaningful work.  她离开银行业去寻找更有意义的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 momentous</td><td>/moʊˈmentəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重大的，重要的</td><td>a momentous decision<br>一个重大的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board reached a momentous decision that changed the company's direction entirely.  董事会做出了一个重大决定，彻底改变了公司的方向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 weighty</td><td>/ˈweɪti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的；沉重的</td><td>weighty matters<br>重大的事务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The elders debated weighty questions of land and water.  长者们讨论着土地与水源的重大问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 10

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>frothy</b></td><td>/ˈfrɔːθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（内容等）欢乐轻佻、不严肃的</td><td>a frothy comedy<br>轻松不严肃的喜剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The theater is showing a frothy comedy about summer romance.  剧院正在上演一部关于夏日恋情的轻松不严肃的喜剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flighty</td><td>/ˈflaɪti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻浮的，反复无常的</td><td>a flighty young man<br>轻浮的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She seemed flighty at first but proved remarkably reliable.  她起初显得轻浮，但事实证明非常可靠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frivolous</td><td>/ˈfrɪvələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻浮的；无关紧要的</td><td>a frivolous remark<br>轻率无谓的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge dismissed the frivolous lawsuit within a single morning.  法官在一个上午之内就驳回了这起无理的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 puerile</td><td>/ˈpjʊrəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>幼稚的，不成熟的</td><td>puerile humor<br>幼稚的幽默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His puerile complaints annoyed the whole committee.  他幼稚的抱怨让整个委员会都不快。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 earnest</td><td>/ˈɜːrnɪst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严肃认真的</td><td>an earnest student<br>认真的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An earnest student stayed behind to ask about the summer reading list.  一名认真的学生留下来询问暑期阅读书目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 serious</td><td>/ˈsɪriəs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严肃的，认真的；严重的</td><td>a serious student<br>认真的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is serious about becoming a research scientist.  她认真地想成为一名研究科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sober</td><td>/ˈsoʊbər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严肃的；节制的</td><td>a sober assessment<br>冷静的评估</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee offered a sober assessment of the country's economic prospects.  委员会对国家经济前景作出冷静的评估。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>frowsy</b></del></td><td>/ˈfraʊzi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的</td><td>a frowsy old coat<br>邋遢的旧外套</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He looked frowsy after sleeping two nights on the train.  在火车上睡了两夜后，他显得很邋遢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slovenly</td><td>/ˈslʌvnli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>邋遢的，不整洁的</td><td>slovenly appearance<br>邋遢的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His slovenly desk made a bad impression on every visitor.  他邋遢的办公桌给每位来访者留下坏印象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unkempt</td><td>/ˌʌnˈkempt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，蓬乱的</td><td>unkempt hair<br>蓬乱的头发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived at the interview with unkempt hair and a wrinkled shirt.  他去面试时头发蓬乱，衬衫也皱皱巴巴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 untidy</td><td>/ʌnˈtaɪdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不整洁的，凌乱的</td><td>an untidy desk<br>凌乱的书桌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His untidy desk was buried under unopened letters.  他凌乱的书桌被未开封的信件埋住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dapper</td><td>/ˈdæpər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>衣冠整洁的</td><td>a dapper little man<br>衣着整洁的小个子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A dapper old gentleman in a gray suit greeted us at the door.  一位身穿灰色西装、衣冠整洁的老先生在门口迎接我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 neat</td><td>/ˈniːt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>整洁的，井然有序的</td><td>neat handwriting<br>整洁的字迹</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her neat handwriting made the notes easy to read.  她整洁的字迹让笔记很好读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tidy</td><td>/ˈtaɪdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>整洁的；整理</td><td>tidy up the room<br>把房间整理干净</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please tidy up the room before the guests arrive.  请在客人到来前把房间整理干净。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spruce</td><td>/spruːs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>整洁漂亮的；打扮整洁</td><td>look neat and spruce<br>显得整洁利落</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old waiter always looked spruce despite his worn uniform.  尽管制服磨旧，这位老侍者总是显得整洁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>frugal</b></td><td>/ˈfruːɡl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节俭的；节省的；朴素的</td><td>a frugal lifestyle<br>节俭的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They led a frugal life and saved almost every coin.  他们过着节俭的生活，几乎每一分钱都存起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sparing</td><td>/ˈsperɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>节俭的，节约的</td><td>sparing use of salt<br>节省地用盐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was sparing with salt because of his doctor's strict orders.  遵医嘱，他用盐十分节省。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 economical</td><td>/ˌekəˈnɑːmɪkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>节约的，经济的</td><td>an economical car<br>省油的汽车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A small diesel engine is more economical on long journeys.  小型柴油发动机长途出行更省油。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 provident</td><td>/ˈprɑːvɪdənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>节俭的；有远见的</td><td>a provident household<br>节俭有远见的家庭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A provident household sets money aside long before hard times arrive.  节俭有远见的家庭远在困难到来之前就存下钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stingy</td><td>/ˈstɪndʒi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>小气的，吝啬的</td><td>stingy with money<br>在钱上很吝啬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is stingy with money but surprisingly generous with his time.  他在钱上吝啬，在时间上却出奇大方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extravagant</td><td>/ɪkˈstrævəɡənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挥霍的；过度的，不必要的</td><td>extravagant spending<br>挥霍的开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their extravagant spending on parties left the charity nearly broke.  他们在宴会上的挥霍开支让这家慈善机构几乎破产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prodigal</td><td>/ˈprɑːdɪɡl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>挥霍的</td><td>prodigal spending<br>挥霍无度的开销</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His prodigal spending emptied the family fortune within a few years.  他挥霍无度的花钱方式几年内就耗尽了家产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sumptuous</td><td>/ˈsʌmptʃuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>豪华的，奢侈的</td><td>a sumptuous banquet<br>一场豪华的宴会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel served a sumptuous banquet for the visiting delegation.  酒店为来访代表团摆了一场奢华的宴会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fungible</b></td><td>/ˈfʌndʒəbl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可互换的</td><td>fungible goods<br>可互换的商品</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Grain and oil are fungible goods traded by weight.  谷物和石油是按重量交易的可互换商品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 interchangeable</td><td>/ˌɪntərˈtʃeɪndʒəbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可互换的，可交换的</td><td>interchangeable parts<br>可互换的零件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two models use fully interchangeable parts.  这两个型号使用完全可互换的零件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 commutable</del></td><td>/kəˈmjutəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可互换的；可减轻的</td><td>commutable into cash<br>可换算成现金</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Under the plan, the pension is commutable into a single cash payment.  根据该方案，这笔养老金可换算成一次性现金支付。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 substitutable</del></td><td>/ˈsʌbstəˌtutəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可替代的</td><td>substitutable resources<br>可替代的资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Coal and gas are largely substitutable in power generation.  在发电中煤和天然气基本可以相互替代。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 switchable</td><td>/ˈswɪtʃəbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>可切换的，可调换的</td><td>a switchable setting<br>可切换的设置</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The desk lamp has switchable warm and cool light settings.  这台台灯有可切换的暖光和冷光设置。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>fullbodied</b></del></td><td>/fʊlˈbɑːdid/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（酒等）味道醇厚浓郁的</td><td>a fullbodied flavor<br>醇厚饱满的味道</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wine has a fullbodied flavor that suits roasted meat.  这款酒味道醇厚饱满，适合配烤肉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 important</td><td>/ˌɪmˈpɔːrtənt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的</td><td>an important decision<br>一个重要的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sleep is as important as diet for a young athlete.  对年轻运动员来说，睡眠和饮食一样重要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 consequential</td><td>/ˌkɑːnsəˈkwenʃəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；随之而来的</td><td>a consequential ruling<br>一项重要的裁决</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That quiet vote proved the most consequential act of the session.  那次不起眼的投票成了本届会议最重要的举动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 momentous</td><td>/moʊˈmentəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重大的，重要的</td><td>a momentous decision<br>一个重大的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The board reached a momentous decision that changed the company's direction entirely.  董事会做出了一个重大决定，彻底改变了公司的方向。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 significant</td><td>/səɡˈnɪfɪkənt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的；显著的</td><td>a significant difference<br>显著的差别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers found a significant drop in the pollution levels.  研究者发现污染水平显著下降。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 weighty</td><td>/ˈweɪti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>重要的，重大的；沉重的</td><td>weighty matters<br>重大的事务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The elders debated weighty questions of land and water.  长者们讨论着土地与水源的重大问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 trivial</td><td>/ˈtrɪviəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无足轻重的，不重要的</td><td>a trivial detail<br>无关紧要的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The editor refused to argue over such a trivial difference in wording.  编辑不愿为措辞上如此无足轻重的差别争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fulminate</b></td><td>/ˈfʌlmɪneɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>大声斥责</td><td>fulminate against corruption<br>大声斥责腐败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old editor fulminated against corruption in every weekly column.  那位老编辑在每周的专栏里大声斥责腐败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 criticize</td><td>/ˈkrɪtɪˌsaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>批评，指责；评论</td><td>criticize the decision<br>批评这个决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Several members criticized the plan for ignoring small farmers.  几名成员批评该计划忽视了小农。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blame</td><td>/ˈbleɪm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>责备，归咎于</td><td>blame someone for something<br>因某事责怪某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't blame me for the broken window.  别把窗户破了怪在我头上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 censure</td><td>/ˈsenʃər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>公开表示反对，谴责</td><td>a vote of censure<br>谴责性投票</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The council voted to censure the mayor for his conduct.  议会投票谴责市长的行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 condemn</td><td>/kənˈdem/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谴责（…为不道德的、邪恶的）</td><td>condemn the attack<br>谴责这次袭击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 World leaders quickly condemned the attack on the hospital.  各国领导人迅速谴责了对医院的袭击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 denounce</td><td>/dɪˈnaʊns/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开指责</td><td>denounce corruption<br>公开指责腐败</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Local leaders publicly denounced the corruption exposed by the newspaper.  当地领导人公开谴责报纸曝光的腐败行为。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 denunciate</del></td><td>/dɪˈnʌnsieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开谴责，指责</td><td>denunciate the regime<br>公开谴责该政权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Speakers denunciated the new law at the crowded rally.  演讲者在拥挤的集会上公开谴责这项新法律。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lambaste</td><td>/læmˈbeɪst/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>严厉斥责，痛骂</td><td>lambaste the government<br>痛斥政府</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics lambasted the film for its shallow, sentimental ending.  批评家严厉斥责这部电影结局浅薄而煽情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 reprehend</del></td><td>/reprɪˈhend/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谴责，指责</td><td>reprehend such conduct<br>谴责这种行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The committee did not hesitate to reprehend the officer for his careless report.  委员会毫不犹豫地谴责了那名官员草率的报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 reprobate</td><td>/ˈreprəbeɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>堕落者，道德败坏的人</td><td>an old reprobate<br>一个老无赖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel's hero is a charming reprobate with no money.  小说的主角是个迷人却身无分文的堕落者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 applaud</td><td>/əˈplɔːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>称赞，赞成；鼓掌</td><td>applaud the decision<br>赞成这一决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teachers applauded the decision to reduce homework for young pupils.  老师们赞成给低年级学生减少作业的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 compliment</td><td>/ˈkɑːmplɪmənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>赞美，称赞；恭维</td><td>pay someone a compliment<br>赞美某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He paid her a warm compliment on her new painting.  他对她的新画作说了一句诚挚的赞美。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 praise</td><td>/ˈpreɪz/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赞扬，称赞</td><td>win high praise<br>获得高度赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her first novel won high praise from serious critics.  她的第一部小说赢得了严肃评论家的高度赞扬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fumble</b></td><td>/ˈfʌmbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>笨拙地做</td><td>fumble for the keys<br>笨拙地摸找钥匙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He fumbled for the keys in his dark coat pocket.  他在昏暗的外套口袋里笨拙地摸找钥匙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 botch</td><td>/ˈbɑːtʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>笨拙地弄糟，搞坏</td><td>botch the job<br>把活儿搞糟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The builders botched the job and had to start over.  建筑工人把活儿搞糟了，只能重来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flounder</td><td>/ˈflaʊndər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>笨拙地行动，挣扎</td><td>flounder in the water<br>在水中挣扎</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swimmer floundered in the cold water before help arrived.  救援到来之前，游泳者在冷水中挣扎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mess</td><td>/ˈmes/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>混乱，脏乱；弄乱</td><td>make a mess of it<br>把它弄得一团糟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was a terrible mess when the children finished baking.  孩子们烤完点心后厨房一片狼藉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stumble</td><td>/ˈstʌmbəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>绊倒，跌跌撞撞</td><td>stumble over a root<br>被树根绊倒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She stumbled on the dark staircase but did not fall.  她在漆黑的楼梯上绊了一下，但没有摔倒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>furor</b></td><td>/ˈfjʊrɔːr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧闹，狂怒；激动，狂热</td><td>cause a public furor<br>引起公众哗然</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The painting caused a public furor when it was first shown.  这幅画首次展出时引起公众哗然。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delirium</td><td>/dɪˈlɪriəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精神错乱；极度兴奋，发狂</td><td>in a feverish delirium<br>处于发烧的昏乱中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For two nights the fever kept him in a restless delirium.  高烧使他两夜都处在不安的精神错乱之中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frenzy</td><td>/ˈfrenzi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狂乱；狂热；疯狂</td><td>in a frenzy of excitement<br>处于极度兴奋中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd worked itself into a frenzy when the singer finally appeared.  歌手终于出场时，人群陷入了一片狂热。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fever</td><td>/ˈfiːvər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>狂热，极度激动；发烧</td><td>election fever<br>选举狂热</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Election fever gripped the small town for weeks.  选举狂热笼罩了这个小镇数周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fury</td><td>/ˈfjʊri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>暴怒</td><td>fly into a fury<br>勃然大怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He flew into a fury when he saw the broken window.  他看到破碎的窗户时勃然大怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hysteria</td><td>/hɪˈsteriə/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>歇斯底里，狂热</td><td>mass hysteria in the crowd<br>人群中的集体狂热</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rumors of invasion produced mass hysteria across the small town.  入侵的谣言在小镇引发集体歇斯底里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outrage</td><td>/ˈaʊˌtreɪdʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>愤怒；暴行；激怒</td><td>public outrage<br>公众的愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The verdict caused outrage among the victim's family and friends.  判决引起受害者家人和朋友的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rage</td><td>/reɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暴怒</td><td>fly into a rage<br>勃然大怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He flew into a rage when the waiter spilled the soup.  服务员把汤打翻时，他勃然大怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 uproar</td><td>/ˈʌˌprɔːr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>喧嚣，骚动</td><td>cause an uproar<br>引起轩然大波</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax plan caused an uproar among small business owners.  新税收方案在小企业主中引起轩然大波。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wrath</td><td>/ˈræθ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>暴怒，愤怒</td><td>incur his wrath<br>招致他的暴怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clumsy apology only incurred his wrath again.  笨拙的道歉只是再次招致了他的暴怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 serenity</td><td>/səˈrenəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宁静，安详</td><td>a sense of serenity<br>一种宁静感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden offers a rare serenity in the middle of the city.  这座花园在城市中心提供了难得的宁静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tranquility</td><td>/træŋˈkwɪləti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>宁静，淡定</td><td>an air of tranquility<br>宁静的气息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He enjoyed the tranquility of the snowy fields at dusk.  他享受黄昏时白雪覆盖的田野的静谧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>furtive</b></td><td>/ˈfɜːrtɪv/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>鬼鬼祟祟的；秘密的</td><td>a furtive glance<br>偷偷的一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He cast a furtive glance at the answers on her desk.  他偷偷瞥了一眼她桌上的答案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 secret</td><td>/ˈsiːkrət/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>秘密的，隐秘的；秘密</td><td>keep a secret<br>保守秘密</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They held secret meetings in the basement for several months.  他们在地下室秘密开会数月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clandestine</td><td>/klænˈdestɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>秘密的，暗中的</td><td>a clandestine meeting<br>秘密的会面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They arranged a clandestine meeting in an empty parking garage.  他们在一个空停车场安排了秘密会面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 covert</td><td>/ˈkoʊvərt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐蔽的，秘密的</td><td>covert operations<br>秘密行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The agency ran covert operations in several neighboring countries.  该机构在数个邻国进行秘密行动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stealthy</td><td>/ˈstelθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐秘的，偷偷的</td><td>a stealthy approach<br>隐秘的接近</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat made a stealthy approach toward the sleeping bird.  猫悄悄地靠近那只睡着的鸟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 surreptitious</td><td>/ˌsɜːrəpˈtɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>偷偷摸摸的，保密的</td><td>a surreptitious glance<br>偷偷的一瞥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He took a surreptitious glance at his watch during the meeting.  会上他偷偷看了一眼手表。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aboveboard</td><td>/əˈbʌvbɔːrd/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无欺诈的，光明正大的</td><td>open and aboveboard<br>公开而光明正大</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole deal was entirely open and aboveboard.  整笔交易完全是光明正大的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fury</b></td><td>/ˈfjʊri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>暴怒</td><td>fly into a fury<br>勃然大怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He flew into a fury when he saw the broken window.  他看到破碎的窗户时勃然大怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 anger</td><td>/ˈæŋɡər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使愤怒；愤怒</td><td>anger the crowd<br>激怒人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His dismissive remarks angered nearly everyone in the room.  他轻蔑的言论几乎激怒了在场所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indignation</td><td>/ˌɪndɪɡˈneɪʃən/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>义愤，愤慨</td><td>public indignation<br>公众的愤慨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ruling provoked widespread public indignation.  这项裁决引发了广泛的公众愤慨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ire</td><td>/ˈaɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>愤怒，怒火</td><td>draw the ire of critics<br>引来批评者的怒火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new tax drew the ire of small business owners.  新税收招致了小企业主的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mad</td><td>/ˈmæd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>疯狂的，荒唐的；愤怒的</td><td>a mad scheme<br>荒唐的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be mad to sell the house at that price.  以那个价格卖房子简直荒唐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rage</td><td>/reɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>暴怒</td><td>fly into a rage<br>勃然大怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He flew into a rage when the waiter spilled the soup.  服务员把汤打翻时，他勃然大怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wrath</td><td>/ˈræθ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>暴怒，愤怒</td><td>incur his wrath<br>招致他的暴怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The clumsy apology only incurred his wrath again.  笨拙的道歉只是再次招致了他的暴怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 forbearance</td><td>/fɔːrˈberəns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>克制；忍耐</td><td>show great forbearance<br>表现出极大的忍耐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher showed great forbearance with the noisy class.  老师对吵闹的班级表现出极大的忍耐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
